The rail asks for every tab's session at its first paint, so a held pane's is read by then. Then `lay_out(map, screen)` | render/questions.rs, wezterm-gui summaries.rs | GUI | per new key or conversation | a lookup | yes |
| 19 | `lay_out`: where the conversation ends (`conversation_end`: the lowest of Claude Code's rules in the bottom 30 rows and the one above it), each row's indent and whether it starts a block, the screen's words (letters and digits, lowercased), a table's rows read a cell at a time (since stage 2, What could go 4: `table_rows` finds each table row, a run of rows whose first ink is `│` under a `┌` or `├` or the screen's top and over a `├` or `└` or the conversation's end, and `screen_words` reads it cell by cell, left to right, each cell's lines top to bottom, as the reply's markdown has them, its words keeping their rows; its lines after the first start no block, so the row is one); then each reply in the file, newest first: each question's first 12 words (at least 2) and each passage's words (at least 3) found as one run of the screen's words, or a part of one cut off at the top or bottom edge (two fifths of it, at least 5 words, in the 4 rows at that edge, judged by each word's row, since a table's later cell comes back up to them); each grown to its block from the highest and the lowest row its words are on (in a table the last word can be above the first), a question's options after it; a marker for each part of a question not found, above or below by its `pos` against what of its reply is found. `Layout::newest` is the file's last reply's questions, on the screen or not | lbterm-extras questions.rs | GUI | per new key or conversation | a search of every quote over the screen's words: the 20 replies' quotes against a few hundred words; not measured | yes |
| 20 | Drawn: a band over each question and passage block (rounded quads in z 23 of the `over` visual, a fifth to a third of its colour) with a bar down its left for each question it's about; the markers' chips (z 24) and words (z 25) at the conversation's top and bottom edges; under a voice draft, ticks and the red ring. Shapes and words kept while the same (`KeptQuads`, `Words`). An empty layout draws nothing (`NoneOnScreen`) | render/questions.rs | GUI | each frame while shown | quads copied from kept ones | yes |

What could go (found in stage 1; each with where it lives and the fix it'd take, for the stages after and for Lachlan):

1. **The reply's questions aren't in the file yet.** Haiku's run puts them there 8 to 19 s after the reply ends (step 3: 12.7 s at the median over ten days), and he reads a reply as it streams, so a hold right after it ends finds only the older replies in the file. With none of them on the screen it shows nothing (`NoneOnScreen`, its `newest` the older reply's labels); held on, the colours come once the file does (steps 4 to 6 bump the generation, step 18 lays out again), but nothing says they're coming. Meanwhile `Layout::newest` is the reply before, so a voice draft's ticks and red ring go by that reply's questions. Shown by questions.rs's test `a_hold_before_the_worker_has_written_the_reply_shows_nothing`. Where: ToolBelt's worker (and Conductor's hub), with the wait in Haiku. Fix: ToolBelt writes the reply's entry at the Stop itself, with no model: the labels (Q1, 2, E4), their first words and where each starts, which the asking rules make findable by rule (`questionsSection` and `MayAsk` already find the section), then adds the passages when Haiku answers, a second rename; the questions pane then lists them at once. In ToolBelt's harness the question block is structure (`QuestionItem`), so there's no wait at all.
2. **The reply never gets an entry.** 23 of 489 asking replies in the ten days to 2026-11-14 had none; the ones read were replies that only said earlier questions were still open ("Q1 to Q4 and E5, E6 from my last message are still open"), which Haiku rightly finds no question in, and a few whose reason isn't known (Haiku's answer, the hub). Where: ToolBelt's worker, Conductor's hub. Fix: the same as 1, labels found by rule.
3. **A fourth quick press went to Claude Code** (fixed in stage 2). The streak never ended (step 8), and lbterm.lua binds streaks 1 to 3 (step 11): a fourth right press on the same cell within 500 ms of the last finds no binding and is Claude Code's, which pastes and shows nothing. Pressing again and again after a hold that showed nothing is how one gets there. From the code; in his window the log's `a right press no binding took` line with `streak Some(4)` says it. Where: LBTerm's click counting (term/src/input.rs, upstream's) and Terminal's lbterm.lua. The fix (stage 2, `config::lbterm::count_click`, called where `mouse_event_impl` counts the press): the streak is held at the longest any mouse binding names for its button, and at a triple click at least, so every quick press past the third counts as the third, which Terminal's binding takes, while a binding of a longer streak still gets its own. Held rather than started again at 1 (as Windows Terminal and Alacritty go round), so what's bound today does the same: a fourth quick left click selects the line again where it did nothing, and the tab bar still sees its double click. config's tests `every_quick_press_finds_a_binding_made_for_streaks_1_to_3` and `a_streak_counts_as_far_as_a_binding_names` failed before it. Terminal's lbterm.lua needs no change.
4. **A passage in a table's cell wasn't found** (fixed in stage 2). Claude Code draws a markdown table with each cell wrapped in its column, so a cell longer than its column isn't one run of words on the screen: it isn't coloured, and its question gets a marker saying it's above, though it's on the screen (25 of 1,921 passages in the ten days were in a table's row; test `a_passage_wrapped_in_a_table_cell_is_not_found`). Where: LBTerm's `lay_out`. The fix (stage 2): Claude Code's renderer (read from its bundle, 2026-11-14) draws a rule under the header and between every two rows (`├─┼─┤`), wraps each cell to its column and centres it in its row's height, and draws a table too wide for the pane in a vertical form of its own (`kind: "vertical"`); so `lay_out` reads each table row (step 19) a cell at a time, its band is the whole table row, and the edge rule goes by each word's row. A passage running from one cell into the next is found too, as the markdown has them in that order. questions.rs's tests `a_passage_wrapped_in_a_table_cell_is_found`, `a_passage_from_one_cell_into_the_next_is_found` and `a_passage_in_a_table_cut_off_by_the_top_still_counts` failed before it. Not covered: a table in the vertical form, whose shape wasn't read.
5. **Nothing of the last 20 asking replies is on the screen.** Scrolled to a part of the conversation with none of their questions or passages, or to a reply older than the 20 the file keeps, the hold shows nothing, as designed, and nothing says so. The questions pane (Lachlan's ask of 2026-11-11) lists the questions whatever's on the screen.
6. **A modifier, or the window without the keyboard.** Step 11 binds NONE only, and step 9 lets no press through without the keyboard. Alt is read from `GetKeyState` at the press, not from the message, so an Alt another program holds down then (a voice tool's or AutoHotkey's) would make it a press with Alt. Not seen; the log's `a right press no binding took` line (`mods`, `allowed false`) says it if it happens.
7. **Work each frame while held that needn't run** (not a cause of a miss): step 15 cloned the pane's whole user var map each frame to read one var (a hold reads it once a press since stage 4; a voice draft still does), and step 6 repaints every window for any session's summary change. The pane the next stages build reads the session once per press.

Ruled out:

- The file's shape: all 539 `questions.json` on the PC that day (3,966 questions) take LBTerm's types (`Map`, `Message`, `Question`, `Quote`); a label with its dot (`Q1.`, 22 of them) shows as written on a marker.
- A rename missed: the worker renames its file into place, and the folder's watch passes the new name on (summaries.rs's test).
- What Claude Code doesn't draw of the markdown: links, URLs and HTML are in 8 of 3,396 question texts and passages.
- `claude_session` empty: claudehook sets it on every working or waiting status, so a reply that asks always comes after it's set; `NoSession` in the log would say otherwise.
- A session not read yet: one frame or two (step 18), with the rail having asked first.

### The pane's rows: what's read for them (stage 3)

The questions pane (QUESTIONS.md, "The questions pane and where its questions come from") takes its rows from `questions::pane::rows(map, turns)`: the session's questions file, already in the summaries store (step 5), and the turns read from its transcript. What runs for them, from the code of feat/questions-pane's stage 3; nothing measured, since this isn't a speed branch:

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | A message to Claude in the transcript (his prompt, a task finishing, another session's message) starts a `Turn`: when it was sent, and for his prompt the questions it names, `drafts::picks_in` over its lines (each line's leading number, options and mark, `pick_at`). Both kinds of record Claude Code writes a message as start one: a `user` record when Claude was waiting, and a `queued_command` attachment when it came while Claude worked, which is all Claude Code writes of it (PITFALLS 361); his queued one adds a prompt's block and its mark on the scroll strip as a typed one does (Lachlan's 7a of 2026-11-14), another session's moves the revision itself, since it adds no block. Each turn says whether it's his (`Turn::his`), for a lone question answered in words (R4) | lbterm-extras `conversation.rs`, `Conversation::user`, `Conversation::attachment`, `turn` | the conversation's reading thread (`Conversations`' `follow`) | per such record, as the transcript grows | a scan of the prompt's lines for a leading number, one small `Vec` when it names any; a push onto `Chunks<Turn>` | yes: the prompt's text isn't kept past it (a block keeps 400 characters, run onto one line) |
| R2 | Each record that changed what a snapshot shows (the revision moved) sets the last turn's `last_ms` to its time | `Conversation::record` | the reading thread | per record read | a compare; a write through `Chunks::make_mut`, which copies the last piece (up to 128 turns, each a few words) the first time after a snapshot took it | yes: it's how a reply's end is told from the turn after it (R4); a record that changed nothing would make the snapshot's turns newer than its blocks |
| R3 | The snapshot takes `turns` with the blocks (`Chunks::clone`, the pieces' `Arc`s) | `Conversation::snapshot` | the reading thread | per snapshot | an `Arc` clone a piece | yes |
| R4 | `rows`: for each reply in the file (20 at most), the first turn after it (`first_after`: a binary search for the last turn that had written by the file's `at`, then back past any that had written nothing since its message); then each pick of each turn from the oldest of those on, given to the newest reply before it that asks its number; then each reply that asks one question and no extra and has no pick for it, answered by his first turn after it if no newer reply had ended by then and it names nothing by number (Lachlan's 6a of 2026-11-14); then a row each question, newest reply first | lbterm-extras `questions/pane.rs` | whoever calls it: the pane, once a press (stage 4) | per call | 20 binary searches over the turns; a pass over the turns since the oldest reply's, each pick against at most 20 replies' questions; a `Row` each question (a few hundred at most) | yes, once a press; not each frame |

The session has to be read for R1 to R3 to have run: the reading thread follows only the sessions a window shows (`wezterm-gui/src/conversations.rs`, `shows`) and keeps those a pane names, so a pane whose session no strip shows needs a `Reader` of its own while it's out.

### The questions bar's paint (stage 4, the bar since Lachlan's words of 2026-11-14)

While a hold shows the colours, each frame that paints them draws the questions bar too (QUESTIONS.md, "The questions bar"), the closed rail mirrored down the window's right. Since E15 (2026-11-14) the colours' layout is worked out first (`lbterm_questions_layout`, kept by its key as before, step 17), so the bar can draw each question's arrow from `Layout::places`; the bar is drawn whether or not the layout found anything, and the edges' markers aren't drawn while it's out. What a press adds, and each frame:

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | The press that shows the colours reads the pane's `claude_session` once (`copy_user_vars`) into `Shown::session`, and tells the conversations' reading thread this window's `Reader::Questions` shows that session (`conversations::shows`: a lock, and only on a change the thread is told, with every session a pane names, a walk of the mux's panes' user vars); the release clears it the same way | `lbterm_questions`, `lbterm_questions_hide` | GUI | per press and release | one user-var map clone; a walk of every pane's user vars when the session shown changes. Nothing at all unless `lbterm_scroll_strip` or `lbterm_context` is set: without one `conversations::folders()` is `None`, `shows` and `get` do nothing, and every row shows open for good (QUESTIONS.md, "What turns the reading on") | yes: the rows need the session's turns, and the thread reads only what's shown. Told only here, at the press: a session no strip follows is read from its start after it, so a first hold on it shows every row open until the whole transcript is read on the reading thread, then one repaint |
| P2 | `lbterm_questions_pane_rows`: `summaries::store()` and its generation, `conversations::get(sid)` (a lock and the config's folders), and the rows kept in `Made` while the session, the generation and the snapshot's revision are the same; else the summary from memory (`store.get`, `None` until read, which asks the thread and draws no card) and `questions::pane::rows(map, turns)` (stage 3's R4), turns empty until the session's been read (on a first hold of a session no strip follows, until the reading thread has read its whole transcript, the read P1 started; its end repaints the window once and the revision it brings makes the rows again) | render/questions_pane.rs | GUI | per frame held; rows made per change of either | two locks and two `Arc` clones a frame; a make is R4's cost, 20 replies at most | yes, the locks to see a change; a make only on one |
| P3 | `pane::place`: the slots' height, as the rail's rows' (`ROW_MAX` and the line under a pad while all fit, shrinking together to `ROW_MIN` and the line), each row's top, the hairlines between replies, the rows that fit and the count of the rest, in the bar's list, from inside its ring under the tab bar to inside it above the window's foot | lbterm-extras/src/questions/pane.rs | GUI | per make of the rows, or a change of the room or the sizes | a pass over the rows (120 at most) | kept since the review of feat/questions-pane (`PaneState::placed`, by the rows' make and the room and sizes' bits): a hold's frames with the same rows place nothing again |
| P4 | Each row's arrow from the layout's places (`Layout::places`, by the row's reply and colour; kept in `PaneState::places` for the slide back), then the bar's shapes (fill, the rail's ring, hairlines, pads, open rings) into z-layer 101 through `lbterm_kept_quads`, kept while the rows' make, the arrows, the bar's place and size, the slot height, the scale, the title font's column and the surface colour are the same; the labels, ticks and "+N" into 102 through `lbterm_draw_words`, each kept while the same | render/questions_pane.rs | GUI | per frame held | a hash of the key and a copy of the kept vertices when the same; a few dozen rounded quads and glyph runs when not. The shapes' list (about 30 entries) is made inside the kept quads' closure, so only when they aren't kept (since the review); the words' list is still made each frame held, a `String` and a rect a row, which `lbterm_draw_words` then finds the same | yes: `over` is drawn whole when it changes |
| P5 | A draft's ticks, and a draft's ring's marker, are right-aligned to the bar's left edge less 6 px (`chips_right`) rather than the pane's right, so the bar never hides one; the layout's markers aren't drawn while the bar is out (E15) | render/questions.rs | GUI | per frame held | a min | yes |

Since stage 5 the pane slides, and these steps follow P5:

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P6 | The bar's visual for the compositor (`PaneState::visual`): its rectangle at rest with the room a card's shadow would take, though the bar has none (`strip::visual_bounds`), what of the window it may show in up to the window's edge, and whether it's coming out; and the session it showed kept (`leaving`) for the slide back | render/questions_pane.rs | GUI | per frame the pane is drawn | a few float ops; the session id cloned into `leaving` only when it's another session's (since the review) | yes: the compositor needs where it is |
| P7 | `prepare_questions_pane`: the first time, its container, clip, slider and surface (a swapchain, made on the GUI thread); then the surface moved or resized only when its rectangle changes (a row added or gone), the clip set only when it changes, and a slide driven only when the hold starts or ends (`drive`, segments sampled from `panel_open` or `panel_close`, from where it is with its speed). While the tab panel's rail is there, the push with it (`Push`, Lachlan's 16 of 2026-11-14): the first time in a hold `push_on` clips `over` to below the tab bar and shows its swapchain in `over_top` above it (an `as_hal` and a QueryInterface for the chain, once a hold), and each time the slide is driven a second `drive` of the same motion `travel` to the left of the bar's, one animation set on the six pushed root visuals (`pushed`), in the bar's commit; `push_off` sets them still at nothing as the bar is put away. A frame held with nothing changed does none of it | compositor.rs | GUI | per composited frame | nothing at rest; two slides' segments when it turns; a swapchain the first hold of a window's life | yes |
| P8 | The card's quads routed to its own visual (`questions_pane::owns_zindex`, render/composited.rs's ranges) and drawn there only when its content key changes (`acquire_questions_pane`): a hold's frames with the same rows present nothing to it | render/composited.rs, compositor.rs | GUI | per composited frame | a key over its quads | yes |
| P9 | Let go (or a focus loss, which ends the hold): the bands go at once; `paint_lbterm_questions_pane_leaving` draws the pane as it was for the session it showed (`leaving`) with `open` false while `questions_pane_showing` says its slide back still shows, which turns the slide round from where it is, and asks for one repaint at the slide's end (`lbterm_questions_pane_wake`), whose paint finds it gone and puts it away. No frame is painted for the slide's steps: DWM plays them | render/questions_pane.rs | GUI | the release's frame, and one at the slide's end | the pane's kept quads and words (nothing new is drawn) and a timer | yes |

The shapes are built into a `Vec` each frame before the kept quads are asked whether they need them: a frame held with nothing changed builds that list (some 30 entries) for nothing. Building it inside `lbterm_kept_quads`' closure would skip it, at the cost of computing the words' places apart from the shapes'.

## The Conductor's state file, from its write to the paint

Since 2026-11-09 (stage 1 of New-tab landing 3, which reads the file's `hidden` here too). Traced from the code (wezterm-gui/src/state_files.rs, lbterm-extras/src/conductor.rs and watch.rs); no step measured on its own. It runs once a write of button.json (`@Conductor:button`): the Conductor writes it whenever its state, mode, listening, asks or hidden list change, on every `conductor hide` or `show` (already so or not), and when its seat starts with it off. A hide or a show is one such write, so Hide from Conductor adds no step and no thread.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Conductor writes a temporary file and renames it over `conductor/button/button.json`, alone in its folder since it moved there (AT-REST.md, "Busy folders") | the Conductor's conductor.ts | the Conductor's | per change of what it publishes | its own | yes |
| 2 | Windows' `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on the folder (and its parent, for the folder's own name) ends; LBTerm's copy of notify passes on only what's about a target, and `Watch`'s callback sends `Wake::Seen` down the thread's channel | deps/notify, lbterm-extras/src/watch.rs | notify's watcher thread | per rename (a temporary file made, written and renamed is several changes) | a wake of notify's thread a change | yes |
| 3 | `lbterm-state-files` wakes on the channel, keeps going only when `watch.matters(&paths)`, sleeps `SETTLE` (25 ms) so the writer's changes come as one, and drains what came meanwhile | state_files.rs `start` | lbterm-state-files | per write | a wake and a 25 ms sleep | the settle: yes, a write is several changes |
| 4 | `files()`: the config as it is now (`config::configuration()`, an `Arc`), and from it the Conductor's `state_file`, the links file and the agenda's | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a config `Arc` and three strings cloned | yes: a config reload can name other files |
| 5 | `watch.follow(&targets)`, up to `FOLLOWS` (8) times while it changes something: the folders watched made right when one was made or removed | watch.rs `follow` | lbterm-state-files | per wake | nothing when the folders are as they were | yes |
| 6 | `look`: each Conductor state file's stamp (`std::fs::metadata`: modified time and size); one unchanged since it was last read is passed over. A changed one is read whole (`read_to_string`) and parsed (`conductor::read`: serde_json into a `Value`, its words, numbers and true or false kept as `Said::fields`, and since 2026-11-09 its `hidden` list as `Said::hidden`, a set of session ids, `None` without a list). A file that doesn't parse keeps what it said last. Then `SAID`'s lock, only to compare what each says with what it said (`Said`'s `==`, every field and the hidden set, `updatedAt` among them) and put it in as an `Arc` | state_files.rs `look`, conductor.rs `read`, `Hidden::read` | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a metadata call a file; for a changed one a read of a few hundred bytes (about 250 with none hidden, and about 40 more a session hidden, its 36-character id) and a parse | yes |
| 7 | `look_links` and `look_agenda`: their files' stamps, each passed over when unchanged | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a metadata call each | yes, as the wake can be theirs |
| 8 | When anything said differs: `repaint_windows` sent to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`, PITFALLS 108), which invalidates every window | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files, then GUI | per write that changed what a file says: every write of button.json, since each has a new `updatedAt` | one task and an invalidate a window | doubtful: every window repaints for any write, though a hide changes what only the windows holding that session's tab show, and a rewrite in the same words (a hide of a session hidden already) changes nothing shown but `updatedAt` (What could go) |
| 9 | Each window's paint reads it again from memory: `lbterm_conductor_said` (render/conductor.rs; `state_files::said`, `SAID`'s lock, a lookup by path and an `Arc` clone, no file) for the pad (`lbterm_conductor_head`), for the Conductor's tab without a seat (`tab_panel_rail`, tab_panel.rs), for the view while it's open (conductor_view.rs), and for the pad's menu while it's open, where an item that waits is answered once what it says differs (`conductor::answered`, so a hide's write answers a waiting mode item too, as any write did before); since stage 2 of landing 3, for the rail's rows too (render/conductor_hide.rs: `lbterm_hides_settle` at the top of `paint_tab_panel` while a Hide or Show waits, and `lbterm_hide_looks` every paint, below) | render/conductor.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, render/conductor_view.rs, render/conductor_hide.rs | GUI | per paint of a window with the pad | a lock and an `Arc` clone a call | yes |

What could go (not decided):

- Step 8: every window repaints for every write, where a hide changes only what the windows holding that session's tab show (the tab menu's row and the mark) and nothing on the pad. Hides are rare (Lachlan's own picks), and the Conductor's other writes change the pad, which every window has; so it's small, and comparing what each window shows before invalidating it would cost more than the paints it saves.

### A Hide from Conductor pick, to the Conductor's word

Since 2026-11-09 (stage 2 of landing 3, with the look and the view's Show from its stage 3 the light up the rail from its stage 4 and Terminal's handler, step 3, from its stage 5; NEW-TAB.md, sections 8 and 9). Traced from the code (render/conductor_hide.rs, compositor.rs's `prepare_lights`, lbterm-motion's send.rs, render/tab_menu.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, render/conductor_view.rs, lbterm-extras/src/conductor/hide.rs, lbterm-extras/src/seat/layout.rs, lbterm-panel's words.rs and layout.rs); no step measured on its own: each runs once a menu open, a pick or a paint, and perf/conductor-hide.ps1 holds the order (the row in the frames after the key, the light up the rail once the card has gone, the glint after the write, the notice no sooner than 5 s). All on the GUI thread but the Conductor's own work and the file's reading (the flow above).

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The tab menu opens: besides the facts of "The tab menu's facts, and a pick from it" (step 1 there), `lbterm_hide_offer`: nothing on the Conductor's own tab; else the file as last read (`lbterm_conductor_said`, a lock and an `Arc` clone), every pane's `claude_session` (`tab_sessions`: each pane's user vars copied, each id once, the active pane's first) and `Picks::offer` (`conductor::offer` on the file's list; `Offer::Waiting` while a pick of the tab's still waits, a greyed row that picks nothing, `menu::inert`, so no second request races the first, PITFALLS 244; Lachlan's 11b of 2026-11-09) into `Facts::conductor`. Asked again only when a pick for the menu's tab begins or settles while it's open (`lbterm_menu_hide_again`, from `lbterm_hide_pick` and `lbterm_hides_settle`: a check of the open menu's tab, nothing more, when it's another's), the greyed row then coming alive in its place | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu`, render/conductor_hide.rs | per right-click on a tab | a user-vars copy per pane; no file read | yes: the row and its words are decided by it, once |
| 2 | H, a click, or Enter on the row: the row flashes as any pick does, the label read again (`tab_label`), the menu closed (its fade, `pane_exit`), then `lbterm_conductor_hide`: the sessions again (`tab_sessions`), nothing more if a pick for the tab already waits (a menu opened before it began; logged, PITFALLS 244), the file's `updatedAt` kept in the `Pick` (`then`), the pick put in `TabPanelState::hides`, the window invalidated, and `lbterm-conductor-hide (window, pane, {sessions, label, hide, source})` fired (`source` "tab menu") with the tab's own active pane (a Lua event, async on the GUI's executor through `with_lua_config_on_main_thread`) | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_menu_pick`, render/conductor_hide.rs | per pick | a user-vars copy per pane, one Lua call | yes |
| 3 | Lachlan's config's handler (Terminal's lbterm.lua, `lbterm-conductor-hide`, built 2026-11-09 on Terminal's feat/conductor-hide), on the GUI thread inside step 2's Lua call: it returns nothing in a GUI with `LBTERM_STATE_FILE` (a test window, PITFALLS 68), so the event goes on to a test's own handler; else it words `--said` from `label`, `hide` and `source` ("picked Hide from Conductor on <label>'s tab in LBTerm", or "pressed Show on <label> in the Conductor's view in LBTerm"), matches each of `sessions` as a full session id (what isn't is logged and left out; each once) and calls `wezterm.background_child_process` once with `conductor hide` (or `show`) `<sid> [<sid>...] --by lbterm --said <his pick> --result <id>`, every id of the pick, and sets `await_result`'s first timer. One call a pick, never one a session: the processes would start side by side, never waited on, and the Conductor's `setHidden` reads hidden.json, changes its one id and writes it back with no lock, so two at once lose one with both results ok (PITFALLS 243; stage 5 built one a session, the review's fix-up made it one). Today's Conductor takes one id a call: two are a usage error with no result written, so a two-session tab's pick hides nothing, step 4 runs it out at 5 s and the notification says at 20 s that it didn't answer, until it ships several ids in one call (asked by LBTerm's InMail of 2026-11-09; NEW-TAB.md, section 8, "What the Conductor shipped"). `background_child_process` (lua-api-crates/spawn-funcs) is `smol::process::Command::spawn` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and stdin null: the process is created on the GUI thread there and then, and never waited on. `wezterm.time.call_after` (lua-api-crates/time-funcs) is a task on the GUI's executor that sleeps on a `smol::Timer` and calls back through `emit_event` only if the config's generation is still the one it was set in (PITFALLS 241); each look is an `io.open` of `<id>.json` in `@Conductor:results`, at 4, 10 and 20 s after the pick until it's there, and deletes what it reads. The Conductor (its own Bun process) writes button.json again with a fresh `updatedAt`, hidden already or not, then its result; the flow above carries the write to every window's next paint, where step 4 settles the pick. A refusal or failure, or no result in 20 s, is one `window:toast_notification`, titled as `hide::not_done` words the strip's notice, with the reason; a call that worked says nothing. With no handler, or one that does nothing, nothing is written and step 4 runs the pick out | Terminal's lbterm/lbterm.lua (`lbterm-conductor-hide`, `await_result`), lua-api-crates/spawn-funcs, lua-api-crates/time-funcs; the Conductor | per pick; the looks one to three, the last 20 s after | one process created on the GUI thread (CreateProcess; not measured), one timer, up to three file opens. Off LBTerm's threads, but inside the 5 s step 4 waits for the write: Bun loading conductor.ts took 260, 297 and 318 ms here (`bun -e require(...)`, the module alone with nothing run, the PC idle, the review of 2026-11-09), then `setHidden` reads the session registry, walks the transcripts of the hidden sessions that aren't running (`gone`, for its prune) and of one being hidden that isn't, and writes two files: measured as a whole by the Conductor (its answer of 2026-11-09, merge 95d0f34): about 100 ms from the command's start to button.json's write, medians of 6 warm runs on a scratch LOCALAPPDATA with the real registry and transcripts (97 ms for one session, 103 for two, 98 for two with four sessions not running already on the list, its walk over 82 transcript folders adding nothing measurable), the process ending at about 115 ms, the PC lightly loaded; more under load and with a cold disk cache (the review's 260 to 318 ms was Bun loading the module alone, on a busier PC), and a pick that meets another's lock waits for its hold, a few ms. So the 5 s leaves well over 4 s for that work | yes: hiding is the Conductor's, and the result is the only place that says why one wasn't done |
| 4 | Every paint of the panel, first thing (panel on or off): `lbterm_hides_settle`, nothing at all while no pick waits. Else the file again (`lbterm_conductor_said`) and `Picks::settle`: a pick the file has been written since and `conductor::confirmed` says is done, its tab id pushed on `TabPanelState::glints` (no invalidate: this is the paint that plays it, `tab_panel_glints` after the rows, once no menu's card holds the rail); one 5 s old (`hide::PENDING`) goes and `lbterm_strip_notice` says `hide::not_done` for 4 s (an invalidate, the strip's wake at its end). Then `hide_due`, the first still waiting's end, which `tab_panel_wake` waits for with the panel's other times and its wake handler counts as due | render/conductor_hide.rs, render/tab_panel.rs `paint_tab_panel`, `tab_panel_wake` | per paint while a pick waits; per wake at a pick's end | a lock, an `Arc` clone and a set lookup a session per pick | yes; nothing polls: a write repaints every window anyway, and only the end wakes one |
| 5 | The rows: `lbterm_hide_looks` over the rail's tabs and the sessions `tab_panel_tabs` already gathered for the hub (none without `lbterm_conductor`), a `Look` a row: a waiting pick's `hide`, else hidden when the file's list names every session. When the file hides nothing and no pick waits, every row's at once without a lookup. Each look goes into `Tab::hidden` before the rows are made, so `Row::hidden` is in both of the row's looks (lbterm-panel's `look.rs`) and the row dissolves on `swap` when it changes, as `RowPlan` does for any look. With the state file on, `tab_panel_said` gives each row `hidden` and `hidden_pending` | render/conductor_hide.rs, render/tab_panel.rs | per paint of the panel | a lock and an `Arc` clone, then a set lookup per session only while something's hidden or waiting | yes: what the rail shows of it |
| 6 | The look drawn (stage 3): in `tab_panel_draw`, while any visible row is `hidden`, the marks' font (`tab_panel_mark_font`: the title font's style at 7.5 pt, 10 px at 96 DPI, kept in `TabPanelState::mark_font` by shape generation and DPI, so looked up once, `lbterm_sized_font` again only after a reload or a DPI change) and `words::marks`, a `Word` a hidden row (nf-md-eye_off, U+F0209, centred on `Layout::hidden_mark`, white at 60% over what's under the pad, `fill` or the card) into `Placed::marks`; `words::place` puts the "Hidden" chip after the project's (an `Area` in `Placed::chips`, its word white at 45% over `CHIP`, only when it fits whole). `tab_panel_pad` cuts the disc (`hidden_mark`, filled in what's under the pad) after the rings and the arc or unread dot, in the rows' quads (and again over an open row's highlight), so `rows_from` (the rows' looks hash, `hidden` in it) makes them again only when a row's look changes. The marks are laid out with the numbers, in the rows' z-layer (with the compositor `ROWS_Z`, so they move and dissolve with their row's visual), and again over an open highlighted row (`raised`); `WordsKey` holds the placed words and `mark_font`, so a frame that changes nothing lays out nothing | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_draw`, `tab_panel_pad`, `tab_panel_mark_font`; lbterm-panel `words::marks`, `words::place`, `Layout::hidden_mark` | per paint of the panel; the font, the words and the quads only when a hidden row's look changes | with nothing hidden, one walk of the visible rows for `hidden`; with a row hidden, a `Rc` clone for the font, a `Word` and a disc a hidden row; the glyph shaped once (the words' cache, render/words.rs) | yes: the mark and the chip are what the hide shows |
| 7 | Show in the Conductor's view (stage 3): the Hidden group at the foot of Sessions (lbterm-extras's `seat::layout`, from `seat.view`'s `sessions[].hidden`; folded until a press, `Ui::is_folded` and `FOLDED_AT_FIRST`) gives each hidden session a Show button (`Target::Show`, its hit pushed after its row's, so the press is Show's, PITFALLS 237); a press runs `lbterm_view_show`: this window's tabs walked for one whose panes hold the session (`tab_sessions` a tab), then, found with a pick of its own still waiting, nothing more (logged: a second request would race the first, PITFALLS 244), found otherwise, a pick for that tab with that session alone (`lbterm_hide_pick`, so its row shows and waits as step 2's does) and its active pane, else no pick and the window's active tab's pane; `lbterm-conductor-hide` fired with `{sessions = {sid}, label, hide = false, source = "conductor view"}` (`lbterm_fire_conductor_hide`, the same Lua call as step 2's). The view stays; the seat's next `seat.view` without `hidden` moves the session out of the group | render/conductor_view.rs `lbterm_view_press`, render/conductor_hide.rs `lbterm_view_show`; lbterm-extras `seat/layout.rs` | per press | a user-vars copy per pane of the window's tabs, one Lua call | yes |
| 8 | The light sent up the rail (stage 4, Lachlan's 6a of 2026-11-01): `lbterm_hide_pick` (steps 2 and 7) pushes the tab on `TabPanelState::sends`, once a tab while it waits and only with the compositor and the panel on (`lbterm_tab_panel.enabled`: nothing else would take it off). A paint with the panel off (its early return in `paint_tab_panel`, after step 4's settle) or without the compositor clears `sends` and `glints` both, the glint step 4 pushed for a pick from the tab bar's menu included: only the composited panel's paint plays them, and before the review's fix-up they stayed queued, de-duplicated, until a reload brought the panel back and then all played at once, stale. The panel's paint, after the rows are drawn, in `tab_panel_glints` once no menu's card holds the rail (`hover.held()`, his 7b: a pick from the tab menu waits out the card's `pane_exit`, so it sets off in the frame the row dissolves into its new look), runs `tab_panel_sends`: the list taken, and for each tab with a row in view on a rail with the Conductor's pad (`layout.head`), `Light::Send` from its pad's glint rectangle (the pad grown 2 px, 6 px corners) to the Conductor's, put on this frame's lights (`lbterm_light_this_frame`: no frame of its own). The compositor's `prepare_lights`, after the paint pass: `lbterm_motion::send` plans it (`None` with motion off), a `Lit` from the lights' pool shows the white swatch through a clip the glint's size, its offset Y driven from the tab's pad to the Conductor's on `send` (X too, which stays: every pad is in the rail's column, the same size), its opacity's segments (up to 35% on `copy_in`, a constant cubic held to the arrival, out on `copy_out` from there) played, the visual added to `panel_lights` and `shown` until its end; the first `prepare_lights` after that, whenever a frame next comes, puts it back in the pool | render/conductor_hide.rs `lbterm_hide_pick`, render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_sends`, compositor.rs `prepare_lights` (`Light::Send`), lbterm-motion's send.rs | once a pick (two picks of one tab under one card send one) | a `Vec` push at the pick; at its frame a position lookup, one plan (cubics a 120th of a second apart: about 72 for the climb and 55 for the light) and three DComp animations (two named `light.send`; X, not moving, is set). Then nothing while it plays: DWM carries it for 860 ms, no frame is asked for and the GUI thread runs nothing for it; its visual stays in the tree at nothing until the next frame after its end | yes: it's the light he asked for (6a); nothing of it runs at rest, per frame or with motion off |

What could go (not decided):

- Step 1 and step 2 each copy every pane's user vars for the sessions (and step 1's other facts copy them again, `tab_session`, `tab_folder`); the paint's `tab_panel_tabs` already has each rail tab's sessions. Keeping them beside `drawn` would drop the copies; it's once a right-click and once a pick, so it's small. Measured whole (2026-11-09, stage 6 of landing 3; PERF.md, "Hide from Conductor, by budgets -Against"): the opening with the Hide row on a twelve-tab rail, `tab-menu-hide-opening`, took 1.43 ms against main's 1.53 to the frame that shows it and 2.92 ms of the GUI thread against 2.98, the same within the rounds' spread (±0.38 and ±0.74), so the copies aren't worth taking out for the opening's sake.
- Step 3 creates the pick's process on the GUI thread (`background_child_process` spawns where it's called, as every handler's start in Terminal's lbterm.lua does); once a pick, so it stays, but a thread for the spawn would take it off the thread that takes his keys. Its result's timers are lost to a config reload in the 20 s after the pick (PITFALLS 241): the hide still happens and the strip still says one that wasn't done, but its reason never comes.
- Step 4 asks the file even when nothing was written since the last paint; a lock and an `Arc` clone, only while a pick waits (5 s at most), so it isn't worth a generation counter.
- Step 6 walks the visible rows for `hidden` every paint of the panel to decide whether the marks' font is wanted; the rows are a few dozen at most and the walk stops at the first hidden one, so it stays.
- Step 7 copies every pane's user vars of every tab in the window to find the session's tab, as `lbterm_view_open_session` does for a session's card; the paint's `tab_panel_tabs` has the rail's sessions already. Once a press; small.
- Step 8's light is planned and handed to DWM in the frame it sets off, then costs the GUI thread nothing; its visual waits in `panel_lights` at nothing until a frame next comes after its end, which may be long after when the window is idle. That's how every light is put away (`prepare_lights`), and a visual at nothing costs DWM a tree node, not a frame, so it stays.

### The hub's word of what the Conductor is on, to the outlines

Since 2026-11-09 (landing 4's stage 2, Lachlan's 7a: the Conductor's pad lights only while it acts; NEW-TAB.md, "Landing 4"). Traced from the code (wezterm-gui/src/conductor_hub.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, compositor.rs's `sync_rings`, lbterm-extras/src/conductor.rs's `Focus`, `outline`, `PadRing`); no step measured on its own. It runs once a `conductor.focus` line: the Conductor's hub sends its whole list of the sessions it's thinking about or acting on (`doing`, an optional `until` in epoch ms) whenever that list changes. Its estimates (`conductor.estimates`, the progress bars) come down the same pipe and go through `set_estimates` instead, not traced here.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `listen`'s `read_line` on the hub's pipe (`hub_pipe`, `\\.\pipe\conductor-hub`) returns a line; `conductor::focus_message` parses it (serde_json) into `Vec<Named>`. The thread started the first time a paint asked `doing`, `acting` or `estimate` (`START`), and reconnects with a backoff when the hub goes, clearing everything (`set_focus(None)`) | conductor_hub.rs `start`, `listen` | lbterm-hub | per line | a parse of a few hundred bytes | yes |
| 2 | `set_focus`: `FOCUS`'s lock; `Focus::next_change` before; `Focus::set` (each named session shown from now or carried on with its new `until`, each one not named marked `gone` now, those past their end pruned), true when what shows changed (a session added, its `doing` changed, one pruned); `next_change` after | conductor_hub.rs, conductor.rs `Focus::set` | lbterm-hub | per line | a few map entries | yes |
| 3a | Changed: `repaint_windows` sent to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`), every window invalidated | conductor_hub.rs, state_files.rs `repaint_windows` | lbterm-hub, then GUI | per line that changes what shows: an outline starting or changing kind, which since landing 4 can light the Conductor's pad in every window | one task and an invalidate a window | yes: the pad is in every window |
| 3b | Not changed but its ends moved (since 2026-11-09, PITFALLS 249): the hub let go of a session, which still shows for its least lap (`ACTING_AT_LEAST`, 1.4 s; `THINKING_AT_LEAST`, 600 ms) and its linger (`LINGER`, 400 ms), or named one again with a new `until`. A task on the GUI thread notifies every window (`TermWindowNotif::Apply`, a second hop, PITFALLS 81) with `tab_panel_hub_wake`: nothing without a rail laid out; else `due.1` (the hub's due) set to `next_change` and `tab_panel_wake` for it. No paint now. Until 2026-11-09 nothing ran here, and an outline let go of stayed until some other paint | conductor_hub.rs `set_focus`, render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_hub_wake` | lbterm-hub, then GUI | per let-go or new `until` | two tasks and a timer a window | yes: nothing else knows the end |
| 3c | Neither: nothing | | | | | |
| 4 | The paint: `paint_tab_panel` asks `conductor_hub::doing` for each rail tab's sessions (a lock and a lookup each) and, since landing 4, `conductor_hub::acting` once (`Focus::acting`: any entry acting and not past its end, here or not) into `TabPanelState::head_acting`, only with a hub configured and the pad on the rail. `tab_panel_rings` takes the pad's outline (`conductor::outline(level, hovered, acting)`: `spectrum` in Accept most and all only while acting or under the pointer) through `PadRing::at`, which keeps the pad's spectrum named in the plan, lit or fading, until a fade out on `panel_glow` would have settled (`compositor::fade_out_lasts`: `panel_glow`'s spring from 1 to within 0.002 of 0, about 540 ms at real speed, worked out from the spring, not measured) and sets `ring_due` for then; each tab acted on or thought about gets its iridescent ring and light. With the state file, the pad's `outline` and `acting`. Then `due.1 = next_change()` (pruning what's gone) and `tab_panel_wake` for the soonest of the panel's dues, `ring_due` among them | render/tab_panel.rs `paint_tab_panel`, `tab_panel_rings`, `tab_panel_head_outline`; conductor.rs `outline`, `PadRing` | GUI | per paint of a window with the rail | a lock a session and one more for `acting`, a few comparisons | yes |
| 5 | The frame's compositor pass: `sync_rings` compares the plan's rings with the visuals it has; the same (an idle repaint) does nothing. Otherwise a ring kept (`RingPlan::same_ring`: the same place, style and light, lit or fading) keeps its visual and its hues where they are, and the pad's fades to 1 or 0 from wherever it is (`Ring::fade_to`, a DComp opacity animation on the plan's curve, `conductor.outline.fade` in the state file's animations); a new one is made (its visual, a hue rotation DWM turns once every 6 s while `spin`, `conductor.outline` in `loops`), the pad's from 0, and one the plan no longer names is removed at once | compositor.rs `sync_rings`, `new_ring`, `Ring::fade_to` | GUI | per frame whose plan's rings changed | a visual or two and an animation; then DWM carries the fade and the hues, nothing on the GUI thread | yes |
| 6 | The wake: at the outline's end, the timer's task on the GUI thread (`tab_panel_wake`'s handler) asks `changes_by(now)` and invalidates; the paint (step 4) finds the outline gone, and for the pad's spectrum in Accept most and all starts its fade out. At `ring_due`, a paint takes the faded ring out of the plan, and the frame removes its visual | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_wake` | GUI | per outline's end, and once more per fade out of the pad's ring | a paint each | yes |

At rest in Accept most or Accept all nothing in this flow runs: no line comes, no wake is set for the hub, and no ring is named, so DWM has no loop of the Conductor's to compose (before landing 4 the pad's spectrum turned there for good, PITFALLS 36).

What could go (not decided):

- Step 3a invalidates every window for any change, and step 3b notifies every window for any let-go. Before landing 4 only the windows holding a session's tab showed its outline; now the pad's spectrum is in every window, so a change of acting needs them all, but a thinking change still repaints windows that show nothing of it. Lines come as often as the Conductor acts, a few a minute at most when it's busy: small next to a frame's cost.

## A prompt Lachlan sends, to the windows that show its time

Since 2026-11-14 (feat/rail-last-input, stages 1 and 2; TAB-PANEL.md, "What a row says", "Since his last prompt"). Traced from the code (lbterm-extras/src/summaries.rs and watch.rs, wezterm-gui/src/summaries.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, lbterm-panel's claude.rs, words.rs and look.rs); no step measured. The time shows on the open panel's rows alone, so a window with the panel shut does nothing for it past step 7's question (but the one whose context pill shows the session, which repaints for its status anyway), and its minutes never wake a closed rail (steps 9 and 10). It runs once a write of `<sid>.prompt.json` in the summaries folder, which the statusline that writes the summaries was asked to make whole through a rename, only when the time changes: once a prompt sent, typed, by voice or from the phone. Nothing in it runs at rest, and nothing in it polls. A session asked for the first time (`Summaries::get`, or `last_prompt`, which asks through it) has every file of it read on the same thread (`Msg::Read`, `Parts::ALL`) and wakes every window (`Changed::Any`), as before.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The statusline writes a temporary file and renames it over `<sid>.prompt.json`, `{ "at": <Unix ms> }` (asked on 2026-11-14, not written by anything yet that day) | the statusline that writes the summaries | its own | per prompt sent | its own | yes |
| 2 | Windows' `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on the summaries folder ends; notify passes the paths on, and `Watch`'s callback sends `Msg::Changed(paths)` down the thread's channel | deps/notify, lbterm-extras/src/watch.rs | notify's watcher thread | per rename (the temporary file's own changes come too) | a wake of notify's thread | yes |
| 3 | `lbterm-summaries` wakes, sleeps `SETTLE` (60 ms) so a writer's files come as one batch, drains the channel, and takes each path's name through `parse_name` under `asked`'s lock: `<sid>.prompt.json` of a session asked for is due `Part::Prompt`; another session's file and any other name (the temporary file) are dropped | summaries.rs `watch` | lbterm-summaries | per batch | a lock, a few suffix matches a path | yes |
| 4 | `read_parts` with the parts due: the known summary cloned (its strings and detail lines copied, its questions', objectives' and tasks' `Arc`s counted up), the file read whole and parsed into a `serde_json::Value` (`read_prompt`, `parse_prompt`). No file is no time; another read error, or a shape that isn't `at` a whole number of milliseconds over 0, is no time with why kept in the summary (`prompt_unread`), logged at warn only when it's news since the last read (`prompt_news`) | summaries.rs | lbterm-summaries | per write | a file opened and read (about 20 bytes), a small parse, one summary cloned | yes; the clone is every part's way (doubtful for a time alone: it copies the detail's lines too, as a cent of `usage.json` does) |
| 5 | Compared with the known summary: the same, nothing more; different only in its cost, its time or why its time can't be read, its session goes on the batch's `usage` list when its cost or its time moved (why it can't be read, changed alone, shows nowhere and wakes nothing); anything else makes the batch `any`. The new summary goes in `known` as an `Arc` | summaries.rs `watch` | lbterm-summaries | per write | two summaries compared, one built with the three fields put in | yes |
| 6 | Woken: with `any`, `Changed::Any` and the generation moved; else `Changed::Usage(sids)` when a cost or a time moved, the generation left alone, since nothing laid out from the summaries (the question colours, the drafts) shows either. wezterm-gui's wake sends `repaint_for` to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`) | summaries.rs `watch`, wezterm-gui's summaries.rs `store` | lbterm-summaries, then GUI | per write that moved the time | a task each wake | yes |
| 7 | `repaint_for`: each GUI window is sent a notify (`TermWindowNotif::Apply`, the `sids` in one `Arc`), and in it `lbterm_shows_usage` (render/context.rs) answers: yes at once when the context pill's session is one (the window's active tab, which repaints for its status anyway when he prompts it there); no at once for a panel shut; an open one has its mux window's tabs cloned out (`Arc`s, the mux's lock let go) and each tab's session found (`tab_session`: each pane's user vars copied) until one is among `sids`, and invalidates. A write that moves the time but not its words (a second prompt within the minute: "1 min" stays "1 min") still paints an open panel once for nothing, since the wake compares times, not words: one paint a prompt, open panel only (the review's probe, 2026-11-14: frame 14 to 15, the row staying "1 min"). Until the review a variant of its own (`Changed::Prompted`, `repaint_prompted`, `lbterm_panel_shows`, a copy of this tab walk) left out the pill's window, and before stage 2 every window holding the tab repainted (`repaint_holding`), a frame that drew nothing new on a closed rail | wezterm-gui's summaries.rs, render/context.rs | GUI | per write that moved the time, once a window | a notify each window; a user-vars copy for each pane of an open panel's window | yes; the paint for words unchanged, no (doubtful: telling the words apart here would make the row's words on the wake, which the paint makes again) |
| 8 | `panel_tab` reads the time from the summary it already got for the tab's label and cost (`crate::summaries::get`: `store()`, the config's `Arc` and `STORE`'s lock; then `known`'s lock, a lookup, an `Arc` cloned), with `Summary::last_prompt()`, a field copied, and puts it in `Tab::prompted` as Unix seconds. `panel_tab` isn't the rail's alone: the strip calls it for the active tab every time it makes its content (`lbterm_strip_content`, each window paint, output at full speed included), the tab menu through `tab_label` (once a pick), the projects card for every tab (`lbterm_projects_counts`) and the new-tab code (`tab_folder`, `lbterm_new_tab_closing`); each pays the copy and a float made, nothing more. Until the review it took a second lookup for it (`summaries::last_prompt(sid)`: the config handle, `STORE`'s lock, `known`'s lock, a hash and an `Arc` again), on every one of those calls (PITFALLS 382) | render/tab_panel.rs `panel_tab` | GUI | per tab a rail paint, the strip's tab every paint, and per call of the others | a field copied and a float made | yes: the row is made whole whatever shows |

| 10 | `words::place` puts the words at the open row's second line's start in `palette::PROMPTED` (Lachlan's 1c of 2026-11-14), the project's chip and the status after them, when the line keeps at least as much room again (else none), and the state file's row says them (`prompted`, lbterm/STATE.md) | lbterm-panel's words.rs; render/tab_panel.rs | GUI | per open row a paint (its words taken from the last lay-out when the same, render/words.rs) | a span and a push | yes |

## A frame, from the invalidate to DWM's present

What runs for one frame of the compositor's window (`lbterm_compositor` on, as in Lachlan's config), from the call that asks for it to DWM's showing it, and how the next frame picks each running motion up again. Traced on 2026-11-10 for Lachlan's 1b of that day, the late start of motion (PITFALLS 304 and 298: DWM starts every animation a frame hands it at that frame's commit, which comes after the frame's curves are worked out, its drawing and its presents), on main's code of that day (c5e60bafb), in three segments: 1, to the curves, from the invalidate to the frame's last curve worked out (`prepare_swap`'s return), from the code; 2, to the commit, from there to `IDCompositionDevice::Commit`, with the surfaces made, sized and dropped inside the prepares, from the code and one run ("The run", below); 3, DWM and the retargets, what DWM does with the commit and how the next paint reads where each motion is, from the code and Microsoft's documentation (fetched 2026-11-10), with no run of its own (the test windows' screen was covered that day). Rows 44 and 64 are the hand-offs between segments. "Found out by" names the code read, the run, Microsoft's documentation ("docs"), a curve worked out, or the doc a number comes from; a cost said as estimated was worked out from the code, and the row says how. A mux pane's frame of output is the pane-output flow's segment 4 (its rows 68 to 105), whose first rows this flow's 2 to 8 share; where a cell here takes a number from there it names the row. CODEMAP.md's "A frame", steps 6 and 7, has where each curve's start is stamped and the draw's order; "What the lag is made of" below adds the rows up.

### The processes and threads it crosses

- `lbterm-gui`, its GUI (main) thread: every step of segments 1 and 2, and every DirectComposition call, the Commit included, through dcomp.dll in its own process (each call goes into the device's batch; nothing reaches dwm.exe before the Commit). It stamps every curve's start in the compositor's model and reads them back at the next paint. NVIDIA's user-mode D3D12 driver (nvwgf2umx) does its submit and present work on this thread (PITFALLS 102). It blocks in the kernel in three places: in an acquire, on a swapchain's frame-latency waitable while DWM still holds both latency frames (not seen); in wgpu-core's `device.maintain(PollType::Wait)` and wgpu-hal's `wait_for_present_queue_idle` (WaitForSingleObject INFINITE on the device's one idle event, PITFALLS 303) whenever a prepare makes, configures or drops a surface; and in `device.poll(Wait)` only with frame dumps on. `lbterm_fading_wake`'s timer, after the commit, fires on async-io's thread (PITFALLS 107) and comes back to the GUI thread as a `TermWindowNotif::Apply`.
- The mux path, for a mux push or a key's switch on a mux domain: the client thread reads the PDU and a GUI-thread task invalidates the window (the pane-output flow's segment 3); a key's switch sends `SetFocusedPane` and `SetShownPanes` to the server from spawned tasks (CODEMAP.md, "A tab switch"), outside this flow's timing. The mux server and the panes' programs play no other part.
- The GPU: one D3D12 direct queue, made once by wgpu-hal's `adapter.rs` (one CreateCommandQueue) and cloned into both `Queue::raw` and `Device::present_queue`. It runs the staging copies, each command list's render passes and the presents queued behind them. Segment 1 reaches it only when a prepare makes or configures a surface, which blocks the GUI thread on it.
- dxgkrnl: `NtDxgkSubmitPresentToHwQueue` and the D3DKMT submits.
- The kernel's DirectComposition channel (win32k): carries the committed batch from LBTerm's process to DWM. Not traced.
- `dwm.exe`, its composition thread: applies each batch at its first frame after the batch arrives and starts every animation in it there, at offset 0; then, every refresh of the fastest screen (160 a second on this PC) while any animation is in any window's tree, samples every animated property at the frame's time less its begin, composes the visuals over each swapchain's latest presented buffer on its own GPU work and presents to each screen at its vblank. Nothing goes back from it to LBTerm for the motion.
- `lbterm-state`, test windows only, after the draw.

### 1. To the curves

| # | Step | Where | Process, thread | Runs | Costs | Needs to run | Found out by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A change asks for a frame: `window.invalidate()` → `lbterm_invalidate_at` (`counters::request` credits the caller's line) → `InvalidateRect(hwnd, null, FALSE)`. A key's tab switch comes through the mux: `activate_tab` or `activate_tab_relative` (a pad's click through `tab_panel_mouse`) → `set_active_tab_idx_without_saving` → the tab left's active pane's `focus_changed(false)` (a ClientPane's focus change on a mux domain, a local pane's focus report; on the GUI thread, before the invalidate) → `invalidate()` → `Mux::notify(MuxNotification::WindowInvalidated)`, which calls every subscriber in turn under the subscribers' write lock → the window's mux subscriber (filtered by window id, its `TabAddedToWindow`, `WindowTitleChanged` and `WindowInvalidated` arm) → a GUI-thread task, `dispatch_notif` → `window.invalidate()` and `update_title_post_status()`. It comes after the presents so that new visuals appear with their first frame and every animation of a layout change starts together (its doc comment; COMPOSITOR.md). The curves themselves need no present, only the commit: they wait for the presents only for that | code |

When a step fails between the prepares and the commit (an acquire's 1 s timeout on the frame-latency waitable, a SurfaceError, any `?` in `call_draw_composited`), it returns with `layers.changed` set and the frames acquired so far dropped (wgpu discards them). `paint_impl` drops the error (`self.call_draw(frame).ok()`), so nothing is logged, and `do_paint_webgpu_checked`'s Lost and Outdated retry applies only to the non-composited path. The tree's changes and the frame's curves wait for the next frame's commit, which `put_away_faded`'s comment relies on (it leaves them to that frame); their model starts stay at the failed frame's moments, so they show late by the time to the next frame.

### 3. DWM, and the motion picked up again

| # | Step | Where | Process, thread | Runs | Costs | Needs to run | Found out by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 64 | **Hand-off, 2 to 3:** `IDCompositionDevice::Commit` (through the `IDCompositionDesktopDevice`, which derefs to `IDCompositionDevice2::Commit`). The whole batch since the last commit goes to DWM in one call: the tree's changes (AddVisual, RemoveVisual), values (`Set*2`), links (content, clip, effect, transform), wgpu's `SetContent` of new swapchains, and each `IDCompositionAnimation` handed to a property since, each a copy taken as it was handed over (COMPOSITOR.md, "The stand-in"), its cubics offsets from the animation's own begin (0 at its first segment; a delay's held segment first when `drive` has one). The presents were queued just before and need no commit. It doesn't wait for DWM (WaitForCommitCompletion is never called). The raw Commit runs in four places: `Compositor::commit`, from `commit_if_changed` (row 63), from `put_away_faded` with no frame (row 80) and from webgpu.rs once after the first surface's `configure`; and `Compositor::with_device`'s own `device.Commit()`, once as the compositor is made, committing the root and its 20 group visuals, before `Compositor::commit` exists and without the lag trace | `compositor.rs`, `Compositor::commit` | GUI, GUI thread; in-process dcomp.dll | each frame whose `layers.changed` is set; each fading wake that puts something away; once as the device is made | not timed alone. Bounded by the run's commits that started nothing, 0.145 to 0.346 ms from a prepare to its commit with the prepares and keys included (frames that committed and started nothing, whatever asked for them: "The run"), so the Commit costs under about 0.1 ms for a small batch (estimated); PITFALLS 304's frames starting nothing 0.15 to 0.24 at the median | yes: nothing set on the tree takes effect without it (IDCompositionDevice::Commit's documentation: the calls are batched and executed atomically, and take effect only at Commit). It's what starts the motion | code; the run; docs |
| 65 | The lag trace, only with `lbterm::compositor::lag` at trace: `Compositor::prepared` taken, `in_flight`'s names whose start is at or after it collected into a Vec, a line "commit X ms after prepare; started: ...", X `prepared.elapsed()` read after the Commit returned | `compositor.rs`, `Compositor::commit` | GUI, GUI thread | each commit, only with the target at trace; otherwise one level check | off, one level check; on, a borrow of `in_flight`, a Vec of names, a format and a line | no in Lachlan's window (a measurement); yes while the lag is measured. How to read it: PITFALLS 328 | code |

| 67 | dcomp.dll hands the committed batch to DWM's composition engine | dcomp.dll (Windows), behind `IDCompositionDevice2::Commit` | the GUI thread making the call; the kernel's DirectComposition channel; dwm.exe | each commit | unknown, Windows' own: not to be told apart from the Commit call without an ETW trace of dwm.exe | yes | docs |

The wait from the Commit to that frame isn't measured: 0 to a refresh of the clock DWM runs at (160 Hz on the main screen, 6.25 ms; 16.7 ms at 60 Hz), and however long DWM takes to pick the batch up | yes. Whether the presents queued just before the commit always reach the same DWM frame isn't known: DWM latches flip-model presents itself, and a present whose GPU work isn't done may show a frame later (PITFALLS 332) | docs |
