### What the lag is made of

A curve's screen lag is the time from the moment the compositor stamped its start to the first DWM frame that includes the commit that handed it over: the stamp to the Commit (rows 11 to 63, measured from prepare's moment by the lag trace) and the Commit to that DWM frame (rows 67 and 68, not measured: 0 to a refresh, and since LBTerm's paints aren't locked to the vblank it changes from commit to commit). A motion from rest shows whole, that much late: no step. A retarget steps, in the old curve's time, by the old curve's screen lag less the new one's. From prepare's moment to the Commit, in the run (rows 19, 20 and 62 have the numbers' sources):

- A frame that commits and starts nothing (whatever asked for it: in a test window most likely the budget runner's or the state probe's; not the put-away, which `put_away_faded` does with no frame, PITFALLS 328): 0.15 to 0.35 ms. That's `prepare` and the other 15 prepares, about 23 content keys and the Commit, with nothing drawn or presented.
- A steady tab switch (16 switches, 1.367 to 2.583 ms, median 1.55): the draw part 1.0 to 1.8 ms where logged (prepare's planning and the curves, the later prepares, the keys and acquires, the uniforms, the upload, the record and the submit), and the lag less the draw part 0.37 to 0.78 ms, median about 0.42: the presents and the Commit less `call_draw`'s preamble before prepare's moment, to within the draw part's 0.1 ms rounding (row 62). How the draw part splits isn't measured.
- A frame that makes a surface: 3.4 to 6.5 ms more for each (the `light.glint` frame, 7.611 ms, made the strip's surface in 5.9).
- The run's first switch, 10.769 ms (draw 7.8, present 3.1, no surface made): most likely a spare's configure (row 20) and the first presents into it, or other first-time costs inside the draw part ("Where the traces disagree").
- A window's first frame (168.975 ms, 31 surfaces) and a resize (126.649 ms, every surface configured) are the same thing at scale.

What has to come before the Commit for a pane's new place to show with its new content: the content keys, acquires, uniforms, upload, record, submit and present of the visuals the commit adds (a pane from the pool or new, a strips' picture), moves together with new content, or whose swapchain the frame made or resized; and, in the prepares, the making or sizing of those surfaces. What doesn't: `lbterm_fading_done` and `menus_fading_until` (read only after the commit); `lbterm_state_visuals`, `recorded`, the slow scope and `FrameDump` (bookkeeping); the surfaces dropped (`trim_spares`, `retire` past the pool); and the keys, acquires, draws and presents of visuals the commit neither adds nor moves, which show at DWM's next composition on their own. The curves need only the commit; they wait for the presents because every animation of a layout change is to start in the commit that shows the new pane.

How big it is on `tab_switch`, worked out from the curve: `cubic-bezier(0.23, 1, 0.32, 1)` over 200 ms moves about 2.2% of its way a millisecond at its start (1/0.23 over 200 ms) and about 1.1% a millisecond 40 ms in (the bezier's parameter about 0.317, dy/dx 2.17). So the measured spread of commit lags, about 1.5 ms, is about 1.6% of the share 40 ms in, and a difference of DWM's frame phase of up to a refresh (6.25 ms at 160 Hz) would be up to about 7% there, if DWM's default begin is quantized to its frames as its documentation implies. Near its end its share is about ((1 − x)/2.04)³, so its 1e-4 end (`NOTHING`) falls about 19 ms before the 200.

### The run

One run, through the warden as a GPU job that only draws, on the measurements' lock (4 CPUs, 34 s, exit 0, at most 604 MB): `budgets.ps1 -Scenario tab-by-key -Runs 15 -Bin <slot>/target/release -SlowEvery -Log 'info,lbterm::compositor::lag=trace,lbterm::start=debug'` on the slot's build of c5e60bafb, on a mux domain, 2026-11-10 at 13:09. The test window's strip was 1114x64 and its pane 1002x641, not Lachlan's 1080 by 1920, so its uploads and presents are smaller than his. Present timing was on, as the budget runner gives every action. `-SlowEvery` logs every part of a paint over 1 ms, the present part charged apart from the draw (`lbterm_extras::slow` takes nested scopes out). The verdict: tab-by-key passed, p50 4.16 ms, p95 6.95, the GUI's CPU 5.94 ms, 1 run of 16 busy (others at 60%). Another window covered the test window's screen (`screen_hidden` and `motion_hidden` 1 in all 17 of the runs measure.json kept), so nothing reached a screen: `after_present_ms` (the presents to the refresh they went up at) and the motion walk's `first_ms` weren't measured. Segments 1 and 3 made no run: no counter, metric or log line times the gaps between prepare's moment and the later ones (composited.rs's "compositor: prepare X, acquire Y" shows only frames over 8 ms, `lbterm::start`'s line only surfaces), `perf/sample.ps1` at the 250 a second or less its doc asks for on the GUI thread (PITFALLS 165) would give about 7 samples for 15 switches of about 2 ms of compositor work each, and what segment 3 turns on is DWM's, which no probe in the build reads.

Surfaces made (`lbterm::start`): 31 in the window's start, each 3.4 to 6.5 ms whatever its size (1x1 swatches 3.5, 3.6, 3.5, 4.8 and 5.2 ms; the 320x525 panel's 3.6 to 6.5; card templates of 844x35 and 35x578 3.4 to 4.3; a 732x521 pane 3.7); after the resize to the test's size 1002x641 in 4.0 and 106x57 in 5.7; later 1114x64 in 5.9.

| Frame | Commit after prepare (ms) | Started | The slow log's parts (ms) |
|---|---|---|---|
| the window's first | 168.975 | none | draw 154.5, present 14.7 |
| the resize to the test's size | 126.649 | none | pass 27.3, draw 106.9, present 20.0; two surfaces made in it |
| the light.glint frame | 7.611 | light.glint | draw 6.9; the 1114x64 surface made in it, 5.9 |
| readout | 1.003 | readout | |
| a new tab's row entering | 1.984 | rail.row.grow, rail.row.enter | present 1.1 |
| outline.arrive | 1.105 | outline.arrive | |
| the first tab switch (the warm-up) | 10.769 | rail.row.swap, rail.marker, pane.dissolve | draw 7.8, present 3.1, pass 0.8; WM_KEYDOWN 14.2 before it (the keyboard layout probed in 12.9 ms, outside the lag); no surface made in it |

The 16 steady switches, each starting rail.row.swap, rail.marker and pane.dissolve, committed 1.850, 1.451, 2.033, 2.113, 1.911, 1.418, 1.508, 1.593, 1.520, 1.817, 1.367, 1.368, 1.573, 1.487, 1.482 and 2.583 ms after their prepares (median 1.55); their draw parts, where logged, 1.3, 1.5, 1.7, 1.4, 1.0, 1.1, 1.1, 1.1, 1.3, 1.0, 1.0, 1.1, 1.1, 1.1 and 1.8 ms (the 1.451 frame's under 1 ms, unlogged), and their present parts never 1 ms, so the lag less the draw, 0.37 to 0.78. A commit after each switch, about 470 ms later, came 0.145 to 0.346 ms after its prepare, starting nothing: a frame that committed and started nothing, whatever asked for it (in a test window most likely the budget runner's or the state probe's). It isn't the put-away: a dissolve's `done` is `at_clock(Leaving::end())`, the curve to `NOTHING`, about 181 ms after the switching frame's prepare; `lbterm_fading_wake` fires 2 ms after it, and `put_away_faded` puts the panes away with a commit and no frame (unless `layers.changed` is set), whose lag line after a frame that committed isn't written at all (`prepared` taken). compositor.rs's module wasn't at debug in the run, so its "panes: put away without a frame" lines weren't there to tell (PITFALLS 328).

What Microsoft's documentation says (fetched 2026-11-10). `IDCompositionAnimation::SetAbsoluteBeginTime`: "By default, an animation function starts when the first frame of the animation takes effect… the first frame that includes the commit samples the animation at offset zero for the first primitive"; the default start "varies depending on the time between when the application creates the animation and calls Commit, to the time it takes the composition engine to pick up the committed changes"; the method "does not control when animations take effect; it only affects how animations are sampled after they start"; the exact time of the next frame is the same as not calling it, a later time delays the start until then, and an earlier one drops the beginning, "sampling starts into the animation function". `IDCompositionDevice::Commit`: the calls "are always batched and executed atomically as a single transaction… take effect only when Commit is called". The animation overview: "Unless otherwise specified, an animation function starts when the Desktop Window Manager (DWM) receives the command to execute the animation." `DCOMPOSITION_FRAME_STATISTICS`: `currentTime` is "as computed by the QueryPerformanceCounter function", `timeFrequency` its units in Hz, `nextEstimatedFrameTime` "the estimated time when the next frame will be displayed". Microsoft's DirectComposition and Windows Animation Manager sample schedules its storyboard at `nextEstimatedFrameTime / timeFrequency` ("schedule the storyboard for play at the next estimate vblank") and never calls SetAbsoluteBeginTime.

### Where the traces disagree, or leave a seam

- **The first switch's lag.** PITFALLS 304 put a run's first switch (13.65 and 9.75 ms) down to "the frame that makes the other tab's surfaces", and segment 1 followed it (up to about 10 ms of surfaces made in `prepare`). Segment 2's run found its first switch (10.769 ms) made no surface: no `lbterm::start` line between the window's start and it. Its extra 6 to 9 ms is most likely a spare popped at another size and configured (row 20), which nothing counts or logs. Other steps inside the draw part can cost their first time as well: the upload of the new tab's whole range (row 56) can need a new staging buffer, which gpu-allocator may serve from a newly made D3D12 heap; the first uniforms and bind groups for new rects (row 54, a make each, the state file's `made`); a sides picture or a swap placed from a spare at another size. A spare of the same size (the tab left's own pane from the warm-up) configures nothing, and nothing logs which spare was popped. The two runs were of different builds (perf/commit-lag's and main's after it landed); settled only once a configure is logged and the warm-up frame's visuals read ("Not traced yet").
- **A surface's cost.** Segments 1 and 3 took 4 to 25 ms a surface from lbterm-motion's `WORDS_DEPTH` note and PITFALLS 298; segment 2's run measured 3.4 to 6.5 ms on this PC's GPU, whatever the size. Whether the upper end is WARP's in the tests or a busier GPU isn't known; the rows use the measured numbers.
- **The strip's words.** Segment 3 has them leading the panes with the strips' pictures; segment 1, from `prepare_swap`'s code, has them starting from their beginning with the panes when switched from rest, and stepping only on a turn round. The code supports segment 1: `prepare_swap` switches its own stack at its own `t` (`Flight::switch(.., t, ..)`) and samples the new leaving at that same `t` (`opacity_segments(i, t, ..)`), where `prepare_sides` samples a stack switched at prepare's `t` at its own later one. Rows 39 and 76 follow it.
- **drive's callers with a delay.** Segment 3 named `face_to` as the only one; the code has two on main, `face_to`'s `hold` and the panel's words on opening (`plan.words_delay`, `PanelLayers::show`), rows 14, 15 and 40. feat/context-bar adds the context bar's, and there makes `Tween::velocity` 0 before a tween's start (`a_tween_waiting_on_its_delay_is_at_rest`), which PITFALLS 337 is about.
- **Where a surface is sized.** Segment 2 listed `Layer::place` and `PaneLayer::place`; segment 3 also had a configure in `move_to`. The code has `PaneLayer::move_to` call `layer.resize` too (a pane's new size in a layout change), row 20.
- **The commit's moment against DWM's start.** Not between the traces but between the docs' model and Windows': PITFALLS 304, the lag trace and the stand-in (`Call::Commit { at }`, `Tree::started`) all take the Commit call as where DWM starts an animation; DWM's documentation says the first frame that includes the commit (rows 68 and 69). The stand-in's model is short by that wait, 0 to a refresh (PITFALLS 326).
- **The seam between segments 1 and 2: the surfaces.** Segment 1 had them as costs inside the prepares' rows; segment 2 traced them through wgpu. Rows 19 to 21 join them, in segment 1's place in the order.
- **The seam between segments 2 and 3: the presents and the commit.** Whether a frame's presents and its commit reach the same DWM frame neither could see (row 68, PITFALLS 332).
- **The pane-output flow's row 95** lists the prepares out of order and without `prepare_new_tab` and `prepare_menus`; `call_draw_composited`'s order is rows 11 to 39's (CODEMAP.md, "A frame", step 7). Row 102 says the commit runs only in a frame; `put_away_faded` commits with no frame too (row 80).

### Not traced yet

- **The gaps between prepare's moment and each later one** (prepare's own length on a steady switch, and each prepare's and drive's and play's moment against it): estimated at tens to hundreds of µs, bounded by the 1.55 to 1.7 ms median commit lag, and up to about 10 ms on a first switch. A trace line under `lbterm::compositor::lag` in `record` (each name's `start − prepared`) and at `prepare_sides`', `prepare_keyboard`'s and `prepare_swap`'s moments, then tab-by-key and tab-by-click again with `-Log 'lbterm::compositor::lag=trace'`, would measure them; it's a change of code, for the build track.
- **How a steady switch's draw part (1.0 to 1.8 ms) splits** between prepare's planning, the later prepares, the keys and acquires, the upload, the record and the submit: `call_draw_composited` holds `prepared`, `acquired`, `gathered`, `recorded` and the submit's Durations; with them, the presents' time and the Commit's own (taken before it and after) on the lag trace's line, one run would split all of it.
- **Which visuals a tab switch draws and presents.** If the pool's last-in pop gives back the surface the tab had, its `Layer::shows` still matches and the pane isn't drawn at all. The state file's `visuals` (drew, kept, uploads, upload_bytes, upload_us, record_us, submit_us, made; STATE.md) frame by frame through a switch, as perf/tab-switch.ps1 reads it, or the drawn count on the lag trace's line, would say.
- **Whether a first switch's extra 6 to 9 ms is a spare's ResizeBuffers,** or the first upload's staging allocation, the uniforms made for new rects, or the sides' and the swap's spares: `Layer::resize` logged under `lbterm::start` with its time, as `Layer::new` is, or configures counted in metrics, and the state file's visuals (made, uploads, upload_bytes) for the warm-up frame, then tab-by-key's warm-up again.
- **How a surface's 3.4 to 6.5 ms splits** between wgpu-core's `maintain(Wait)`, wgpu-hal's `wait_for_present_queue_idle` and DXGI's `CreateSwapChainForComposition` (or `ResizeBuffers`) and the three GetBuffers: each timed apart in `Layer::new` and `Layer::resize`. Row 20's configure cost, and row 19's reading of where a make's time goes, rest on it.
- **Which commits wrote the run's "started: none" lines:** compositor.rs's module at debug (its "panes: put away without a frame" line) beside the lag trace.
- **The first Present into a swapchain just made or resized** against a steady one (the first switch's present part 3.1 ms, the start's 14.7 ms for about 31 visuals, against about 0.42 at steady switches): `Frame::present` timed a visual, `shows` None against Some.
- **The Commit's own cost:** bounded under about 0.1 ms by the 0.145 ms frames, not timed alone.
- **Acquire waits** on the frame-latency waitable in fast back-to-back frames (Lachlan's `max_fps` 120 on screens of 160 and 60 Hz): not seen in the pane-output flow's run 4, not looked for here.
- **The time from the Commit to the first DWM frame that includes it, and what time DWM uses for that frame** (its composition time or its estimated display time; Microsoft's sample treats it as the next vblank, which is inferred). A probe build reading `IDCompositionDevice2::GetFrameStatistics` (`currentTime`, `nextEstimatedFrameTime`, `lastFrameTime`) just before and after each commit, logged with the lag trace, in a test window whose screen isn't covered; or `DCompositionGetFrameId(COMPOSITION_FRAME_ID_CREATED)` before the commit and the next frame's statistics. Then a `-Slowdown 5` capture of a fade from rest, to see whether the curve's first visible value is offset 0 at that frame.
- **Whether DWM honours a begin time in the past on this PC's Windows build, and how far back,** and what a property shows before a begin in the future (the documentation says only that the start is delayed): a probe build giving one fade `SetAbsoluteBeginTime(qpc_of(prepare's now))`, compared frame by frame against one without, at `slowdown = 5` with captures (motion/capture.ps1), the screen uncovered.
- **Whether a frame's presents and its commit always reach the same DWM frame** (whether "a pane's new place shows with its new content" holds frame by frame): present.rs's SyncQPCTime for the pane's present against the commit's first frame from the probe above.
- **How the commits fall against DWM's vblank,** so the real spread of screen lags between two frames: LBTerm paces paints from a paint's start on a high-resolution timer, not on the vblank. Needs the probe above.
- **DWM's own cost a property or an animation in the tree,** apart from the rest of the desktop (COMPOSITOR.md's "Loops" couldn't separate it): an ETW trace of dwm.exe on a quiet desktop.
- **The first paint of a tab just shown after a hold, step by step** (row 6, PITFALLS 329): 15 to 25 ms for perf/mux-hidden.ps1's 36-row screen of links in run 13 of the pane-output flow, of which the link scan of a screen it hadn't scanned is about 3 ms (MUX.md, "Tabs out of view"); the rest (laying out lines never drawn, their quads, the upload) isn't split, and nor is what a real Claude Code tab's costs. It's what makes the frame wait's split a frame period: the split itself happens in Lachlan's window (12 waits, each a kept frame, 17.3 ms on average, from 13:00 to 14:26 on 2026-11-10 by `metrics`' `frame_wait`), the strip's swap starting at the kept frame as the code has it, not seen in a capture.
- **The connection lamps** (row 35): whether their laps, modelled at `prepare_connection_lamps`' moment while DWM starts them at the commit, step when a lamp is handed over again mid-run (`pointer_came`); `lbterm_extras::glow` and `render/connections.rs` weren't read. A line's laps restart from its middle, not from where the light is (COMPOSITOR.md), so they aren't a retarget from a value.
- **The hearts** (row 36): how long a burst waits between the trigger that raised it and the frame that hands it over (`lbterm_hearts_plan`, render/triggers.rs, not read); latency only, since hearts don't turn round.
- **feat/context-bar's `prepare_context_bar` and `acquire_context_bar`:** only its moment, its place in the order and its delayed drives were read in its diff; any Tween or play it adds stamps its start by one of rows 40 and 41's paths.
- **Not read whole:** `sync_rows`, `sync_marker` and the panel's `show` and `wave.stop`; they use drive's Tweens and prepare's moment as rows 14 and 15 say.
- **That std's `Instant` is QueryPerformanceCounter on Windows** is from std's source as known, not checked in this toolchain.

### What could go

The flow as it should be, designed from the rows above (2026-11-10), for Lachlan to decide before a track builds any of it. What he asked for: what DWM shows matches what the compositor worked out, for every motion, with no step at a retarget, the side strips with the panes, and the first switch to a tab never shown included; no pane's new place showing before its new content; no fallback path; no cost on a frame's hot path.

What makes the steps, from the rows: each curve's model start is stamped at one of about twenty moments in the frame (rows 11, 15, 22, 26, 28 to 41), DWM starts it at the first DWM frame that includes the commit (rows 64, 68, 69), and every retarget, end, wake and hold reads the model (rows 73 to 80), never DWM (row 72). The time between the two is latency on a motion from rest, which shows as nothing, and a step whenever two frames' lags differ, which they always do: prepare to Commit 1.4 to 2.6 ms on a steady switch and 9.75 to 13.65 on a first (rows 19, 20, 62), the Commit to DWM's frame 0 to a refresh (row 68), the larger and more variable part. So the fix makes DWM's start the model's; shortening the lag alone leaves it different from frame to frame. Largest saving first.

1. **One moment a frame, and every animation given it as its begin time** (weighed as (a)). Rows it restructures: 11 (prepare's moment becomes the only one), 15 (`sync_rings`' own), 22 (`rise`'s stamp), 26 and 28 to 39 (each prepare's own moment), 40 to 43 (drive's and play's stamps, `record`), 69 (DWM's default start); with them rows 73 to 80 read a model DWM plays to.
   - `prepare` takes the frame's moment once, as now, and keeps it with its QueryPerformanceCounter count in a Cell on the Compositor for the frame. Every prepare after it reads that moment instead of an `Instant::now()` of its own: `prepare_sides`, `prepare_keyboard`, `prepare_hover`, `prepare_strip_card`, the seven `prepare_faded` calls, `prepare_menus`, `prepare_connection_lamps`, `prepare_hearts`, `prepare_lights`, `prepare_readout`, `prepare_swap`, `sync_rings`, and feat/context-bar's `prepare_context_bar`. Every from-value, flight `t`, chain and `done` is then worked out at it.
   - `drive` stamps its Tween at the frame's moment plus its delay, the moment its caller read the from-value at, not at its own `Instant::now()`; `play`'s `record` takes the frame's moment; `FocusOutline::rise` stamps prepare's moment, the one its segments were sampled from; `MenuSpinner::turn` stamps it as `started`.
   - One maker of animations, `Compositor::animation()`: `CreateAnimation`, then `SetAbsoluteBeginTime(the frame's QPC count)`, before any segment and long before the hand-over (PITFALLS 294, 334). All seven makers go through it: `drive`, `play`, `lap`, `Spinner::turn`, `new_ring`'s hue rotation, `prepare_connection_lamps`' y animation and `MenuSpinner::turn`. A delay stays what it is, a held segment from offset 0, now counted from the frame's moment.
   - The QPC count: one QueryPerformanceCounter read beside `epoch` as the Compositor is made (`with_device`), and each frame's count worked out from its Instant (`epoch_qpc + (at − epoch) × frequency`), the bridge `present.rs`'s `Now` already makes (`qpc_of`, PITFALLS 336), moved to where both use it.
   - Saves: every step. DWM samples each curve at its frame's time less the moment the model says it began, so at every DWM frame the screen shows the model's value at that frame's time. A retarget reads the model at its frame's moment and starts the new curve there from that value and speed; DWM plays the old curve up to the new commit's first frame and the new one from the same moment, so they meet (rows 73 to 76). The strips' pictures and the strip's words play the flight at its own time whatever moment they were sampled from (row 26, PITFALLS 298, 325). The first switch to a tab never shown plays on the same time as every other, whatever its 10 to 14 ms of surfaces and presents (rows 19, 20). The `done` moments, `put_away_faded`'s wake, `settles`, the state probe and present.rs's Episode become DWM's own ends (rows 79, 80, PITFALLS 327). A failed paint's curves, committed a frame later, play from their own frame's moment ("When a step fails"). It costs one in-process COM call an animation, batched in dcomp.dll like the AddCubic calls beside it (well under a µs, estimated), and a multiply; it takes about twenty QueryPerformanceCounter calls a frame away. Nothing on the hot path. The decision moments (rows 7, 27, 31a, 46) stay their own: they decide, and read the same model DWM then plays.
   - What Lachlan would see: every turn round (a tab switched back to while it fades, a split or close retargeted, a card or menu opened while it closes, the panel's words, the keyboard's light) goes on from where it was, where today it steps by what the motion moves in the difference of two frames' lags (about 1.5 ms of the curve, 1.6 to 3% of a switch's fade 40 ms in, up to about 7% with DWM's frame phase). The strips no longer lead the panes. And every motion's first DWM frame shows its curve as far in as that frame comes after the frame's moment, where today it shows its start, so each motion ends that much sooner (a 200 ms fade shows for 180 to 198.5 ms): on `tab_switch`'s ease-out the first frame of a steady switch, 1.4 to 8 ms in, shows 3 to 17% of the fade where today's shows none (today's first frame is offset 0, a frame with nothing moved), and the first switch to a tab not shown before, 10 to 20 ms in, shows 21 to 40% (change 2 takes most of that away). Short curves lose more of their start: a 60 ms pulse up to about an eighth of its time on a steady frame, a third on a first switch's.
   - Risks: whether DWM on his Windows build honours a begin in the past, and how far back, isn't tested (Microsoft's documentation says an earlier begin samples into the curve; "Not traced yet"), so a probe comes before the build. An animation made without the one maker plays on DWM's default start again, which the stand-in refuses (below). A begin in the future doesn't arise: every begin is the frame's moment, before its commit. What stays: `MenuSpinner::hold` sets a value, which shows at its commit's DWM frame, so the held arc still steps back by the stop frame's screen lag, about 0.4° to 2°, as its card fades out; holding it with a short curve to a stop, given the frame's begin, would take that away too (a small extra). The frame wait's split (row 6, PITFALLS 329) is two frames by design and stays.
   - Tests: the stand-in (`compositor/recorder.rs`) records `SetAbsoluteBeginTime` as part of the animation (in the copy a property takes, as segments are), and its `commit` starts each fresh animation at its begin, turned back to an Instant through the same bridge, the commit's moment only bounding when it shows: `Tree::shows(t)` the old state before the commit and the curve at `t − begin` after. It fails a commit that hands over an animation with no begin time, so a maker that skips `Compositor::animation()` fails every test. `compositor/imp/tests/flight.rs`'s lags (`Shot::take`'s `started − alpha.start`, and the sides' `started − (done − length)`) come out 0, so its allowance for the spread of lags, at a switch and between strips and panes, goes to 0: the strips held to the panes' share within `OPACITY_EPSILON` at every 2 ms and a switch's step to what the flight itself moves, the check PITFALLS 304 says passed only under the wrong model, now the right one. A stand-in DWM frame phase (the first frame at the commit plus 0 to 6.25 ms, swept) shows no step either way. PITFALLS 296's and 297's rules for timing tests hold as they are.
   - Measure: before building, one probe build giving one fade `SetAbsoluteBeginTime(prepare's moment less 50 ms)`, read at DWM's frames (perf/screen-counts.ps1, or motion/capture.ps1) in a test window whose screen isn't covered, to see DWM sample 50 ms into it. Before and after: a turn round at real speed read frame by frame on the screen (tab-switch.ps1's step 6e top line, side-strips.ps1's 3b strips against panes, with screen counts), whose step should go from the lag difference to nothing; tab-by-key and tab-by-click (gui_ms, p50, p95: unchanged); `lbterm::compositor::lag` (prepare to Commit is latency and doesn't change; its line can add each animation's begin less the commit); flight.rs's lags (0).
   - Size: compositor.rs on the code side (the frame's moment through about twenty prepares and helpers, the seven makers through one, the stamps of `drive`, `play`, `rise` and `turn`), the QPC bridge shared with present.rs, recorder.rs (one method recorded, the commit's start rule, the refusal) and flight.rs's allowances. No upstream file. feat/context-bar's `prepare_context_bar` and its delayed drives change with it, so it lands after that branch, or that branch's lines change in the merge.

2. **Surfaces made and sized outside the frames that start motion.** Rows it takes out of those frames: 19, 20 and 21 inside a prepare, and the first switch's extra 6 to 9 ms if it's a spare's configure (row 20, "The run").
   - The pools kept at the sizes the next frames will want: the pane pool's spares, the sides' and the words' sized to the current layout's panes and strip in `put_away_faded`'s commit with no frame (or a task after it, once nothing fades), and spares made when a tab opens or the window resizes, so a switch pops a surface already its size and configures nothing. Drops (`trim_spares`, `retire` past the pool, `put_away_sides` and `sync_swaps` past theirs) only in `put_away_faded`, which already does that work with no frame (row 21's no). A first card, menu or hover of a kind still makes its surface in the frame that first shows it, once a window.
   - Saves: 3.4 to 6.5 ms of the GUI thread a surface, and an idle wait a drop, out of the frames that start motion; a first switch's commit from about 11 to 14 ms after its prepare to a steady switch's 1.5, if the warm-up's time is the configure. With change 1 that's latency and a smaller first-frame skip only, since the screen follows the model anyway; without change 1 it narrows the first switch's step and the strips' lead, and leaves every other step.
   - Risks: the trace hasn't shown where a first switch's time goes (`Layer::resize` isn't logged, "Not traced yet"), so that's measured first. A surface sized ahead for a layout that changes before it's used is sized again. Memory: a spare at a pane's size is what the pool holds today, but a spare made ahead for a tab not yet switched to adds a swapchain of three buffers, about 25 MB at 1080 by 1920 (his lean is speed over memory, so it's said, not asked). Lachlan would notice the first switch to a tab starting as soon as later ones do, and with change 1 nearer its beginning.
   - Size: compositor.rs's pools (`Layers::spare`, the sides', `SwapStack`'s) and `put_away_faded`, a debug line on `Layer::resize` with its time; a compositor test that a switch between tabs of the same layout configures nothing. No upstream file.

3. **What's read only after the commit, read after it.** Rows 45, 46, 51 and 61's bookkeeping (`lbterm_fading_done`, `menus_fading_until`, `lbterm_state_visuals`, `GpuWork::recorded`, the debug line) run before the commit and are read only after it. Saves under a few µs a frame of the lag; with change 1 the lag no longer shows, so nothing changes on the screen. Not worth a change of its own; it goes in with change 1 if that touches the lines. Size: a few lines of composited.rs.

Weighed and not taken:

- **(b) Motion committed apart from content, before drawing.** A commit after the prepares with the animations and values, and another after the presents with the tree's changes and the new content, would take rows 45 to 62 out of the curves' lag (prepare to Commit from about 1.5 ms to the prepares' 0.15 to 0.35). DirectComposition commits its whole batch at once, so it means two batches, and on a tab switch the tab left's panes would start dissolving in the first over the next tab's, whose spare swapchains still hold another tab's last frame until the second's presents (row 49): the uncovering before the new content, which the goal rules out. The two batches can land in different DWM frames (PITFALLS 332). And it leaves the Commit-to-DWM wait, the larger and more variable part, so every step stays: shorter lags, still different from frame to frame.
- **(c) Curves worked out for the expected commit moment.** Each frame's curves stamped at a forecast of its first DWM frame (prepare's moment plus the expected draw and present, or `IDCompositionDevice2::GetFrameStatistics`' `nextEstimatedFrameTime`) instead of when they're worked out. Every miss is a step again: a frame that makes a surface misses by 3.4 to 6.5 ms, a first present into a new swapchain by more, and DWM's frame phase, which LBTerm's paints aren't locked to, by up to a refresh. With a begin time too, a forecast later than the commit's first frame holds every curve at its from-value until then (a pause at each retarget), and one earlier is change 1 with a guess in it. It adds a call for DWM's statistics each frame.
- **Reading DWM back and moving the model onto its start** (frame statistics after each commit, the stamps shifted to the frame they report): DWM reports frames after the fact and never which frame took a commit, so the model would follow an estimate, a second clock kept for what change 1 gets from one call an animation.
- **Presenting the visuals the commit neither adds nor moves after it** (rows 49, 50 and 62's no): about 0.3 ms a present out of the lag, which change 1 makes invisible, against an acquire's pairing (PITFALLS 333) and a frame's content and its tree landing in different DWM frames (332).
- **Starting the rail's, the strip's and the cards' motion with the panes' in the frame wait** (row 6, PITFALLS 329): not the commit's lag but a frame apart by design while a held pane's push is awaited; change 1 keeps each on its own frame's moment, and moving them is a change to the frame wait for Lachlan to ask for on its own. He asked for it to be looked at on its own (his Q4 of 2026-11-10, yes). What the frame period is made of: the push comes 0.8 to 3.7 ms after the switch's first try (run 13's whole-window build), but the kept frame is drawn, so the pacing holds the panes' frame a period after it (16.7 ms on his 60 Hz screen), and that frame paints the new tab for the first time since its hold, 15 to 25 ms for a screen of links. The routes to one start, from the rows and run 13: the whole window held for the push (built in perf/hidden-output's first round) holds the rail for that first paint too, 16 to 30 ms later on screen, which his 2b of 2026-11-08 chose against; the panes' dissolve begun at the kept frame's moment (change 1's begin in the past) skips its first period, against his 2b of 2026-11-10; the strip's words moved to the panes' frame was asked on 2026-11-09 and kept as built (the pane-output flow's "Decided by Lachlan on 2026-11-09", (Q2)). The one route that would cost nothing seen is a hold of the switch's first frame for the push only as long as it can still be drawn for the same DWM frame (the probe's forecast of that frame, fix/motion-begin, less the frame's draw), which pays only once the new tab's first paint is about a steady frame's cost; at 15 to 25 ms it can't. So it starts with a trace of that first paint ("Not traced yet"), not a change to the wait.

### Earlier findings, before the rows

CODEMAP.md's "A frame" has the path, COMPOSITOR.md's "How a frame reaches the screen" the compositor's part, and these found what a frame did that it needn't:

- perf/frame-loop (2026-10-30): the GUI thread spun between frames, since a held `WM_PAINT` left its region invalid and Windows sent it again at once. A held paint is now validated and a high-resolution timer ends the hold, and the message loop waits with no timeout (`window/src/spawn.rs`, `run_until_message`; CODEMAP.md, "A frame", step 2). Frames come when they're due: a period from a paint's start, `max_fps`' 8.3 ms or the screen's refresh if that's longer.
- perf/lbterm-layers (2026-10-31): LBTerm's own drawing laid its cards', lists' and rail's words out again every frame; now it does so only when what they show changes, kept by `kept.rs` and `render/words.rs` (a frame with the InMail list open over a busy tab, 47 to 4.6 ms of the GUI thread; with the projects card, 18 to 4.6). PERF.md, "What the cards cost".
- perf/paint (2026-11-01): a frame keeps what didn't change. The card, ring, glow and lit-ring templates are built once each time what they're made from changes (`render/kept.rs`), each layer keeps one GPU buffer and only the quads whose hashes differ are written to it (`termwindow/gpu_work.rs`, `WebGpuVertexBuffer::upload`; `lbterm_motion::content::dirty_runs`). COMPOSITOR.md's "What a frame costs" paragraph.
- perf/lbterm-layers too: every line a pane draws went past LBTerm's own looks at it (the triggers' rules, the thumbnails' look for a picture's link, the link under the pointer) before its cached quads could answer; now a line is laid out again only when it changed (PERF.md, "What a line costs"; the state file's `work.lines` counts the misses).
- Work on a pane's rows stays out of `with_lines_mut`, which holds its terminal against its parser: copy what's needed (`render/pane_lines.rs`) or read what's cheap (PERF.md, findings 37 and 69; CODEMAP.md, "Hot paths").
- Frames missed while motion plays: PERF.md, "Frames missed during motion".

What these left unknown, a frame's steps and what DWM does after the commit, is the rows above (2026-11-10); a busy frame's draw part is still measured whole, not step by step ("Not traced yet").


## The context bar's widening and its pane's opening, from the pointer's rest to the end of their slides

What runs when the pointer comes to rest on the context bar (lbterm/CONTEXT.md, "At rest and widened", "The pane"), on the second round's design (Lachlan's 4b of 2026-11-09: the bar itself widens, a pane as wide as it beside it for the thin sections' numbers), from the code of feat/context-bar's second round, stage 4. All on the GUI thread but the slides, which DWM plays. The costs are the budget runner's per action (`context-bar-widening`, `context-bar-pane-opening`; PERF.md, "The budget runner"); no step was timed on its own.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The pointer's move onto the bar: `lbterm_context_bar_mouse` takes it (the bar and the gutter left of it), sets `pointer_on`, tells the `widen` Hover the pointer is there (and `pane` when it's on a section), and invalidates the window for the ring's light | render/context_bar.rs `lbterm_context_bar_mouse`, from mouseevent.rs | once a move | a few compares | yes |
| 2 | Frame 1: the paint pass; `paint_lbterm_context_bar` finds the open tab's session and the context `Arc` and lays nothing out (`LaidFrom` unchanged), and draws the bar's four shapes and its runs in `over`, the ring lit; `paint_lbterm_context_widened` ticks the Hovers (nothing open yet) and starts a timer for the rest's end (`lbterm_context_bar_wake`: an async `smol::Timer` on the GUI thread's executor, its expiry a `TermWindowNotif::Apply` that invalidates) | render/context_bar.rs, render/paint.rs | once | part of the action's GUI thread (5.9 to 7.3 ms over its three frames, row 7) | yes |
| 3 | The rest's end (`widen_delay_ms`, 150): the timer's notify invalidates; frame 2's paint ticks `widen` open, so `held` and `widened` are true; `CardsFrom` differs, so `lay_out_drawer` runs: `context_bar::runs` again for the widened track with a line's floor (a few dozen sums), `context_bar::numbers` (the on-section and thin numbers, the thin ones placed as `labels` places them), and each number measured by `compute_element` through `Widths` (about a dozen words, none the second time unless the font or the sections changed). The pane isn't laid out: until the second round's stage 4 this frame laid out the pane it didn't show as well (about forty words measured and every path cut by halving, `fit_end`) | `paint_lbterm_context_widened`, `lay_out_drawer` | once a widening | a few ms at most, the measures | yes, the bar's own layout; the pane's moved to row 8, where it's shown |
| 4 | Frame 2 draws the widened bar's card, track, runs and words into `DRAWER_Z`, and the thin numbers' pane's into `THIN_Z` (the cards' quads by `lbterm_menu_rounded`, the words by `lbterm_draw_words` with their kept quads) | `lbterm_context_draw_drawer`, `lbterm_context_draw_thin` | each frame while out (none at rest) | quads and words for about 15 numbers | yes |
| 5 | The compositor (render/composited.rs, after `prepare`): `prepare_context_bar` with the three `Slide`s and the ride. The first time any is wanted in a window it makes all four visuals and their swapchains together, in their order (the warm-up run's GUI thread was 33 ms against 6 to 8 after it). Then the bar's slide starts on `panel_open` from its home (its left edge on the bar's) to 0, and in the same `drive` the one animation goes to the thin pane's `Slid::follow` as well, the visual its container (and so its clip, which ends on the widened bar's left ring) is in, so DWM moves that clip with the bar's moving left edge; the thin pane's own slide inside it (its width, out past its clip's right edge, to 0) and the riding strip's glide (from its own place to the thin pane's left edge) start in the same commit on `panel_open` with no wait, so the thin pane comes out from under the bar's moving edge and the strip rides its left edge, one motion settled when the bar is (since round 4's stage 6, Lachlan's E9 of 2026-11-10; until then the thin pane's and the strip's waited for what was left of the bar's, `drive`'s `delay`). No `CreateAnimation` of its own: the follow takes a copy of the bar's as it's handed over | compositor.rs `prepare_context_bar`, `Compositor::drive`, `Slid::make`'s `follows` | once a widening; the visuals once a window | a few DComp calls, one `SetOffsetX` more than before; the visuals' and swapchains' making once | yes |
| 6 | The frame's textures: each wanted layer whose content changed renders its z-layers into its own swapchain and presents it (`Layer::frame`'s `must_draw`; an unchanged one isn't presented again), then the window's own present and the commit. What each frame presents of the bar's four, read from a build that logs it (round 3's stage 1, 2026-11-10, `probe context presents` lines): the widening's frame the bar's and the riding strip's, and the thin numbers' pane's only on a window's first widening, since its swapchain still holds what it showed at the last (`Slid::hide` empties the clip and leaves the swapchain and its `shows` key as they were, and the thin card lays out the same each time); the pointer's leave the bar's (its ring no longer lit); the thin pane's close the strip's (drawn at the widened bar's edge); the narrowing's frame none of the bar's (its content `CardDraw::Last`, the same key); the pane's frame (row 8) the pane's and the strip's. Since round 3's stage 2 the branch logs it, at debug under `lbterm::present` (`context presents bar, ride`, from `acquire_context_bar`), with each slide's start and delay and each part put away (`prepare_context_bar`), a level check when the log doesn't ask; walk-read.ts sets them among a walk's refreshes | `acquire_context_bar`, render/composited.rs | each frame while the visuals are wanted | the GPU's; not measured | yes, once a change |
| 7 | DWM plays the bar's slide, and in it the thin pane coming out from under the bar's moving left edge (its clip in `Slid::follow`, moved on the bar's own animation) and the strip gliding on the thin pane's left edge, all from the widening's commit: one motion, which settles 336 to 341 ms after the widening's frame on Lachlan's `panel_open` (round 4's stage 6, below; 648 to 652 ms in the same windows on 0ffd7619b). Until round 4's stage 6 it was the bar's slide, then the thin pane's and the strip's, about 340 ms each, so the widening settled about 840 ms after the pointer came (842 ms median at 19:28, against 503 for the first round's drawer, which slid once). The GUI paints nothing for it meanwhile: 3 frames an action, the ring, the widened bar, and the state file's, which only a test window paints. A slide coming out wakes no frame at its end (PITFALLS 219). In runs of the 4-miss kind (PITFALLS 306) DWM gave the screen no present for the 4 refreshes after the first, its own frames going on at 160 Hz: the window hung past its screen's edge and the slide's first 33 px happened where no screen shows them (round 4's stage 5, below; PITFALLS 351). In a window all on its screen it misses 1, as main's motions do | DWM | once a widening | none of LBTerm's | yes; see What could go |
| 8 | The pane's opening: resting on a section, the `pane` Hover's rest (`open_delay_ms`, 300 from the arrival, so 150 ms into the bar's slide) wakes frame 3, which lays the pane out (`lay_out_pane`: the kinds' column, the buttons, Compact now, each row's tokens and its path cut by `fit_end`, about forty measures the first time), takes Compact now's look at the tab's screen (`lbterm_context_compact_look`: the pane's `claude_status`, and when that isn't working its last rows read), draws the pane into `PANE_Z`, and the compositor starts the pane's slide and turns the ride's glide to the pane's edge from where it is. This frame lands while the bar's slide is still playing, and its presents (the pane's swapchain, the window's) go up while DWM animates the bar | rows 3 to 6 for the pane | once an opening | the action's GUI thread 10.9 to 11.7 ms over its five frames in the first round's runs | yes, the pane's layout and look; when its frame lands is the rests' (150 and 300) |
| 9 | Going back (since round 3's stage 3, 2026-11-10): the frame `held` ends (the `widen` Hover's grace up, the pane shut and in) is one commit for all of it. `lbterm_extras::context_bar::out` turns `held` and the compositor's `showing` into what the paint draws: the widened bar and the thin numbers' pane from their last frame (`CardDraw::Last`) from that frame to their slides' end, whatever a layout would say (none runs: `lay_out` is `held`), the card taking the pointer until its own slide begins (`held || showing[3]`), and the strip riding the card's edge only while `held`. `prepare_context_bar` goes through the parts thin pane first when the bar goes back (`back`), so the thin pane's slide in on `panel_close` is started first and the bar's (`Slide::after_thin`) waits for what's left of it (`slide_wait`'s `thin_left`, 150 ms; `drive`'s `delay` holds its start value until then), the thin pane's clip going with the bar's narrowing through `Slid::follow` once it's in, which shows nothing. That's only from out (Lachlan's 3a of 2026-11-10, kept by round 4's stage 6): turned round while the bar is still coming out (its tween not done, `slide_wait`'s `bar_moving`), the bar goes back at once with the thin pane, each from where it is with its speed, since a delayed drive holds its from-value and the bar would stop dead mid-slide for 150 ms (PITFALLS 353); round 3 never met it, its thin pane not being out until the bar's slide was done. The strip goes home from the thin pane's edge with it, 121 px on `panel_close` in its own visual, under the widened bar for the last 54 (`ride_own`'s `was_away` keeps it in its visual on the frame it starts home), as it came out. `going` wakes once, at the bar's settle (`settles`, the last of their motions), and that frame puts the three away and draws the strip in the window's layer where it already shows. A tween waiting on its delay is at rest (`Tween::velocity`), so the pointer back on the card in those 150 ms opens it again from rest, with no rest waited out: the `widen` Hover's closing lasts one slide (`set_motion`'s `panel_close` duration), which is the thin pane's (perf/context-bar.ps1's 2f). In the bar's own 150 ms after it the card no longer takes the pointer and that closing is over, so only the bar at rest takes it, and the rest is waited out again, as before round 3. Before, the bar's slide waited for the compositor to say the thin pane was in (`widened = held || showing[3]`): a wake at the thin pane's settle, then a frame and a commit of its own 25 to 40 ms after it (the main layer presented with the strip drawn at home, the ride put away), and the strip went at once from the card's edge to its own place under it | render/context_bar.rs `paint_lbterm_context_widened`; compositor.rs `prepare_context_bar`; lbterm-extras context_bar.rs `out`, `slide_wait`, `ride_own` | once a close | a frame and a commit for both slides, and the one at the end; before, a wake, a frame and a commit more between them | yes |

Where the refreshes without a present fall, traced on 2026-11-09 at 21:48 to 21:57 (the PC unlocked, the displays on, the right-most screen clear, others on 36 to 62% of the job's CPUs; a copy of the branch's build at f760e4786): `budgets.ps1 -Scenario <s> -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` for `context-bar-pane-opening`, `context-bar-widening` and `cost-pane-opening`, each episode's frames read out of the window's log by DWM's present count and vblank time on DISPLAY1 (60 Hz; "A test window's motion", below). Main's motions miss exactly one refresh, the second after their first frame (LBTerm's Later, PERF.md "Measured on a window that could be seen"). Against that:

Reading a run's trace: each round walks its episodes in order, told apart by their refreshes (DISPLAY1 at 60 Hz). A `context-bar-widening` round has three: the widening (39 to 41 refreshes: the bar's slide on `panel_open`, the thin pane's delayed slide after it, to the settle), then the thin pane's close (9 or 10), then the narrowing (8). A `context-bar-pane-opening` round has the pane's opening (39 to 44: the widening and the pane's slide together), then the pane's close (9 to 11), the thin pane's close (10) and the narrowing (8 or 9); a `cost-pane-opening` round the fade-in (13 or 14) and the fade-out (7 or 8). In the window's log (the newest `lbterm-gui.exe-log-<pid>.txt` under the run's data folder), each episode starts at its `episode ... opened at Q settling at Q, base frame Some(<id>)` line; the `frame <id> start ... presented <count> <refresh> <vrefresh> <time>` lines after the base frame give each frame's start in ms from the opening, DWM's present count (its step from the line before is how many presents that frame got) and the refreshes by vblank time, and a refresh between two presents with no present of its own is a miss ("A test window's motion", below, has the fields; stage 6's reader of them stayed in a scratchpad).

- **The widening's start: 4 refreshes, every run.** The frame of rows 3 to 6 goes up (a present 2 ms either side of the episode's opening), then 4 refreshes come with no present at all (at 15, 34, 53 and 65 ms; DWM has nothing outstanding for that screen), then two presents land in one compositor frame at about 77 ms and every refresh after has one, to the settle at 650 to 670 ms: the bar's slide, the thin pane's delayed one and the strip's ride play without another miss, the hand-over at about 340 ms included. In the widening alone (`context-bar-widening`, median `missed` 5 of 39 to 41) these are all of them; in the pane's opening the same 4 or 5 come first. A PrintWindow capture at real speed (`perf/context-bar.ps1 -Capture`, `motion-open`, the bar's left edge found in each frame by its amber section) has the edge move 5, 10, 11, 10, 6, 4, 3 px about 20 ms apart from about 45 ms after the widening's frame, a spring's start from rest with no jump; PrintWindow has DWM draw the window's tree at the call, so it says where DWM's animation was, not what went to the screen. So the slide isn't late in DWM's tree, but nothing of it reached the screen for about 70 ms. This reading took the widening's frame to present three swapchains with fresh content (the widened bar, the thin numbers' pane and the riding strip); round 3 found it presents two, the bar's and the strip's, the thin pane's only on a window's first widening (row 6), and that the 4 refreshes didn't come again on this build or any other (round 3's reading, below).
- **The narrowing's tail: 5 to 7 of its 8 refreshes, every run.** Going back, the bar's slide on `panel_close` (`150ms cubic-bezier(0.23, 1, 0.32, 1)`, an ease-out quint, about 54 px) has a present at each of its first 2 to 4 refreshes (to about 36 ms), then none to its settle at 148 ms. By the curve, 80% of the distance is done in 40 ms and the last 70 ms move it under 3 px, a pixel at most every other refresh, which is where the misses fall; the capture of the close (`motion-close`) has the edge at 43, 27, 20, 17 and 15 px from about 15 to 90 ms, so DWM's tree still moved in the span the screen got nothing. The thin pane's close on the same curve over about the same distance misses 1 or 2 (its start), presenting through its tail. Why the two differ isn't known: what the bar's tail changes on screen is its left edge's column and its numbers, a pixel at a time, where the thin pane's text moves whole. And the tail isn't all of it: the review's walk at 22:19 (6 rounds on a copy of the build at 299a4727f, others on 18 to 41%) had the narrowing miss 6 or 7 of its 8 refreshes, 3 of the 6 with only 1 or 2 presents in all, so refreshes in its first 40 ms, where the bar moves several pixels each, went without presents too. Whether the bar's visual is put away, or its content left as it was, before its slide ends was the next thing to look at: round 3 found neither (below), and the 6 or 7 didn't come again. What the code says so far: `prepare_context_bar` (compositor.rs, the loop over bar, thin and pane, about line 6788) hides a part at once, its clip emptied, on the first frame its `Slide` is `None`; the bar's `Slide` is `None` whenever the paint has no `drawer` (render/context_bar.rs, `state.visuals`, about line 747; `drawer` comes from `card_draw` on `widened` and the compositor's `showing[1]`, about lines 680 to 700, not read for this); and the bar's close starts only once the thin pane's is done by the compositor's word (`widened = held || showing[3]`, about line 604), `context_bar_showing` giving each part's settle from its motion.
- **The pane's opening adds none of its own.** Its frame lands 150 ms into the bar's slide and the bar keeps presenting across it. Its run (`context-bar-pane-opening`, median 9.5 of 39 to 44) had single misses about every 100 ms after the start's (at 101, 201, 332, 433, 532 ms and the like), but its windows' rail motion in the same run had them too (8 of 55, at 106, 307, 457, 657 ms), and that run's PC was the busiest (others on 52 to 62% of the job's CPUs in 5 of its 9 runs): the PC's, not the bar's. The widening alone, on a calmer PC, had none past its start.
- **The cost pane's fade: 1, the motion's start** (13 to 21 ms, as main's), 0 in its first run; its close the same. Stage 6's 4 of 15 (20:53, the PC busier) isn't the pane's either.
- **Why stage 6's widening walked no episode** isn't known: this run walked one every run (`missed` measured, 5). Every run then had the screen clear too.

Round 3's stage 1 traced both again (2026-11-10, 00:47 to 00:55, the PC unlocked, the displays on, the right-most screen clear from 00:47; from 00:37 to 00:45 Lachlan's try window, Discord and DashboardsBox covered all of it, so those runs walked nothing and aren't counted; others on 12 to 18% of the job's CPUs, the clock 2.5 to 2.7 GHz). Three builds in turn, `budgets.ps1 -Scenario context-bar-widening -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` each, read with `bun .claude/tools/walk-read.ts` (perf-scripts.md): the branch merged with main at 31d805ddb with the probe's debug lines and nothing drawn ahead ("the branch", 3 rounds), the probe (probe/context-bar-widening's ef94e7619, never landed: the thin pane and the strip drawn and presented on the ring's frame, the thin pane's content at its clip's right edge and the strip at its own place, neither moving, so the widening's frame presents the bar's swapchain alone; 3 rounds), and 8ff322c6a, the branch before the merge (2 rounds). Each round has 7 widenings (the warm-up's with them), so 21, 21 and 14.

- **The widening's start: 1 refresh, as main's, in these runs only.** (Overturned by round 3's stage 2 and stage 5: every one of these runs was of the kind that misses 1, and the next kind, where the widening's start misses 4 or 5, came in 4 of stage 2's 8 runs and in every window of stage 5's 03:01 to 03:28 with others on only 10 to 18% of the job's CPUs, so neither "as main's" nor "the PC's" below holds for the branch; see stage 2's bullets.) The branch's frame went up at -15 to +4.5 ms of the episode's opening and missed the refresh after it (1 to 28 ms from the opening, a refresh after its present) in all 21, and one more in 8 of them, at 48 to 65 ms or at the hand-over to the thin pane's slide (345 and 378 ms): `missed` 1 in 13, 2 in 8. 8ff322c6a the same in the 10 of its 14 the walk based right (up at -13 to -1 ms, missed 1 or 2). The 4 refreshes with no present after the frame of 2026-11-09 21:48 came in none of the 56 widenings walked, on any build, 8ff322c6a included: they were taken here for the PC's then (others on 36 to 62% of the job's CPUs), not the design's, which stage 5 put out (above).
- **Whether DWM holds the commit until every swapchain in it has presented: no.** The probe's widening frame presents the bar's swapchain alone (its lines say so, every run) and went up no sooner: at +7.8 to +30.2 ms in the 14 of its 21 based right, a refresh after the branch's, often with two presents in one refresh and then two refreshes with none (at 33 to 64 ms), `missed` 1 or 2 by the walk's count, as the branch's. Presenting the strip's and the thin pane's ahead buys nothing; the redesign it was for (What could go, below) comes out.
- **The walk counted a present from before the episode in a third of the probe's and 8ff322c6a's runs, and in none of the branch's.** In 7 of the probe's 21 widenings and 4 of 8ff322c6a's 14, the first present the walk took as the motion's going up was the ring's frame's, 125 to 175 ms before the opening (`first frame up at -168.3 ms`), so every refresh of the rest's 150 ms counted as missed (`missed` 9 to 11); the same in the thin pane's close in 11 of the probe's 21 and 6 of 8ff322c6a's 14 (the pointer's leave's present, 250 to 280 ms before; 14 to 16). The budget runner's median `missed` flipped with them: 8ff322c6a 6 in one round and 1 in the next, the probe 1, 5.5 and 1.5, the branch 1, 1.5 and 1. In those episodes the walk's base frame was the frame before the one that showed the earlier present, the compositor having made no frame between it and the opening: `COMPOSITION_FRAME_ID_COMPLETED` names the newest frame only once a later one has begun, so on a quiet PC the base is a frame older than the last present (PITFALLS 301). Why the branch's 42 episodes never met it isn't known: the three builds ran in turn, a minute apart, under the same load.
- **The narrowing: the bar's visual is neither put away nor drawn again before its slide ends.** (The flow before round 3's stage 3; row 9 has it as it runs since.) The code (`paint_lbterm_context_widened`): the narrowing starts on the frame the thin pane's close settles, `going` having woken it at that settle (`widened = held || showing[3]`, `showing[3]` false once the thin pane's tween is done); the bar's `Slide` is `open: false` from then and its content `CardDraw::Last` (the last drawer, the same quads, so `Layer::frame` presents nothing); the next wake is the bar's own settle (`showing[1] && !widened`), and only then is `drawer` `None`, the `Slide` `None`, and `prepare_context_bar` hides it (`Slid::hide`, its clip emptied). The probe's lines, the same code: `context.widen closes from 0.0 to 54.0`, then `context.widen hidden, its slide 0.0 ms from its end` 179 to 190 ms later (the 150 ms slide and the wake after it), no present of the bar's swapchain between; the riding strip is put away on the narrowing's frame (its glide home was done with the thin pane's close) and drawn at its own place in the window's layer, under the bar's visual until the bar's edge passes it.
- **The narrowing's misses: the second refresh in about half, and its tail in all.** 3 to 5 of its 8 to 10 refreshes in all 50 walked (21 on the branch, 17 on the probe, 12 on 8ff322c6a), the same on every build: the refresh after its first present (at 19 to 42 ms) in 25, as main's motions miss, one at its opening in 6, and the last 3 or 4 before its settle (from 84 to 96 ms on) in every one. The curve says where: `panel_close`, `150ms cubic-bezier(0.23, 1, 0.32, 1)` over 54 px, is at 51.4 px at 69 ms, 53.0 at 85, 53.7 at 102, 53.9 at 119 and 54.0 at 135, so from about 100 ms the bar has less than half a pixel left to go. The thin pane's close, on the same curve over 79 px, has 3 to 4 px left in the same span and gets a present at every refresh of it (its 1 or 2 misses are its start's: a 2-refresh gap at 22 to 38 ms). So DWM composes the slide at whole pixels and makes no frame where the bar's offset rounds to the pixel it was at: the tail is refreshes with nothing new to show, not frames lost. An inference from the two slides on one curve; DWM's documentation wasn't read for it.
- **The review's 6 or 7 of 8, 3 of 6 rounds with 1 or 2 presents** (2026-11-09 22:19) came in none of these: every narrowing had 4 to 6 presents.

Round 3's stage 2 walked both again (2026-11-10, 01:22:27 to 01:26:45, the PC unlocked, the displays on, no run covered; others on 9 to 37% of the job's CPUs by the runner's column; another session's `perf-claude-working` test window opened on the screen at 01:22:37, in the first run). Eight runs in turn, 8ff322c6a (`target/before-round3`) and the branch at e303f1f20 (the walk's paint cut by the vblank time, which PITFALLS 302 shows wrong, so its own `missed` isn't counted here), `budgets.ps1 -Scenario <s> -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` each, 7 episodes a run with the warm-up's; every episode read again from its raw frames with walk-read.ts by the target's present time (PITFALLS 301 and 302), which reads both builds' logs the same way but for the paint cut, which only the branch's have. - **The widening's start misses 4 refreshes in some runs, on both builds and in both scenarios, and in every round of such a run.** 4 of the 8 runs, minutes apart: the frame of rows 3 to 6 goes up (DWM's present to the screen 1 ms after the opening, the count up one), then the screen's vblank time moves four times (16.6, 33.3, 50.0 and 66.6 ms) with the count still and nothing outstanding: DWM made no present to that screen for four refreshes (the raw frames of 01:24:04, in the run at 01:23:51), so the walk is right there, and nothing of the bar's slide reached the screen for about 70 ms. Then two more go at about 95 to 130 ms, and the rest of the episode presents at every refresh. This is the review's "4 to 5 at its start" and 2026-11-09 21:48's 4; stage 1's runs (12 to 18% others) were all of the other kind, so it took them for the PC's load then.
- **In those runs it's the bar's visual alone.** Its narrowing misses 5 to 7 there (1 to 4 in the other runs), from about 55 ms on and not only the tail; the thin pane's close and the pane's close in the same windows, a second after each widening, miss 0 or 1 (in every one read of the last four runs, the two branch runs': 7 thin pane closes, 9 pane closes and 7 thin pane closes), and the rail's motion at each window's start 1, as main's. The last four runs, the PC busiest (39 to 54%), had it in every widening of both builds and both scenarios, and the budget runner's median `missed` read 4.5 on both builds for the widening and 5 on both for the pane's opening; the branch's own walk (the cut by present time) said 4 or 5 a widening, as walk-read.ts did from the same frames, its first frame up within a refresh of the opening every time (no present from before the paint taken). So it isn't the screen missing frames at random: something about those runs hits the bar's motions, its opening with a fresh present of its swapchain and its close with none, and not the other parts' (the thin pane's and the pane's visuals, under it in the z-order).
- **What the condition is isn't traced.** Not the build or the scenario (both have it and both don't), and not the CPU's load: in these eight runs it went loosely with it (no run at 9 or 16% had it, 4 of the 6 at 23 to 37%, all 4 at 39 to 54%), but in round 3's stage 5 (2026-11-10, 03:05 to 03:21, `budgets -Against`, 20 windows of the two scenarios, 8ff322c6a and the branch in turn, the right-most screen clear) every window was of this kind, 300 runs missing 4 or 5 (3 twice, 6 twice) with others on only 10 to 18% of the job's CPUs, the PC 15 to 17% busy and the clock 1.45 to 2.2 GHz (PERF.md, "The context bar's motion, by `budgets -Against`"). The other GPU jobs at the time (the warden runs up to 3 `--gpu-draw` jobs at once, and another session's test windows ran in these minutes) weren't read: the warden's `jobs-history.jsonl` had no line for these minutes when looked at, nor any after 01:26 that night when stage 5 looked, so it isn't where a GPU job beside a run can be read.
- **The presents each frame makes are as stage 1 had them**, now from the branch's own lines (`context presents ...`, kept since this stage): the widening's frame the bar's and the strip's, the pane's frame the pane's and the strip's; and the slides' starts with their delays (`context context.thin opens from 79.0 to 0.0, after 311.1 ms`, the ride's the same) and each part put away (`context context.widen hidden, its slide 0.0 ms from its end`).
- **Every other widening start misses 1, as main's motions are counted to,** and that one may be the walk's own arithmetic, not a refresh without a new image ("A test window's motion", What could go; PITFALLS 302).

Round 3's stage 3 redesigned going back (row 9) and walked it (2026-11-10, 02:09 to 02:28, the PC unlocked, the displays on; others on 13 to 43% of the job's CPUs by the runner's column; `budgets.ps1 -Scenario <s> -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` through the warden with `--gpu-draw --lock lbterm-measure`, read with walk-read.ts). The branch at dbc3186a3 and 8ff322c6a (`target/before-round3`) in turn: `context-bar-widening` four times each way and `context-bar-pane-opening` twice, one of the branch's widening runs with 3 of its 7 rounds covered (not counted); and a probe build (never landed) with the branch's flow, once plain, once presenting the bar's swapchain in the commit of its slide back and twice playing that slide on a linear curve (one of them covered). Going back is now one episode (`context.thin, context.widen, scroll.ride`, about 300 ms: the thin pane's 150, then the bar's 150), where before it was two (the thin pane's close, then `context.widen` alone). The runs were of stage 2's two kinds, told by the widening's start (4 or 5 missed, or 0 to 2), named below the 4-miss kind and the other kind (stage 3 called them busier and quieter, but stage 5 found the 4-miss kind with the CPU quiet, so the CPU doesn't tell them apart; PITFALLS 306):

- **In the runs of the other kind (the widening's start missing 1) the bar's slide back now presents through to its settle in most.** The bar's part of the branch's going back missed none in 9 of 13 (the episode's misses, 0 to 2, all in the thin pane's first 40 ms or at 83 ms, as its close's were) (5 of 7 in the probe build's plain run at 02:20, 4 of 6 in the pane-opening run at 02:26), and 3 or 4 at the bar's tail (from 236 to 253 ms, about 90 ms into its slide) in the other 4. 8ff322c6a's narrowing missed 2 to 4 at its tail (from 85 to 101 ms on) in 22 of 24 (the runs at 02:11, 02:14, 02:25 and 02:27) and none in 2. The thin pane's part missed as its close did before.
- **In the runs of the 4-miss kind, no change.** The bar's slide presents for its first 50 ms or so and then nothing to its settle, 5 to 7 missed, on both builds (the branch's at 02:09, 02:13 and 02:24, from 198 to 215 ms of its going back; 8ff322c6a's narrowing at 02:09 from 56 to 65 ms), while the thin pane's part before it presents through. This is stage 2's condition, the bar's visual alone, and its cause still isn't traced.
- **The tail isn't refreshes with nothing new to show** (stage 1's inference, from the curve's last half pixel). On the branch the same eased tail, sub-pixel steps included, presents at every refresh in most episodes of the other kind; and the probe's linear slide back (6 px a refresh to its end) in a run of the 4-miss kind (02:18) stopped presenting about 96 ms into its 150, with about 19 px to go. Where the presents stop goes with the run's kind, not with how far the bar has left: as if DWM played the bar's slide some 50 ms ahead of the walk's refreshes in a run of the 4-miss kind and 10 to 20 in one of the other, which would put its last whole-pixel change where they stop. Not measured: nothing reads where DWM has a visual at a refresh.
- **Presenting the bar's swapchain in the commit of its slide back changes nothing.** The probe's run with it (02:19, of the other kind) missed 0 in 4 of 7 and 3 or 4 at the tail in 3, as the branch's plain flow did.
- **What the redesign took away** is the bar's own frame and commit at its start: the window's layer presented with the strip drawn at home, the ride put away, and the bar's slide starting 25 to 40 ms after the thin pane's settle, on a wake. Which of those made the old narrowing's tail miss in runs of the other kind isn't known; the probe says it isn't a present of the bar's own.

Round 4's stage 4 probed the bar's own swapchain through each episode (2026-11-10, 13:56 to 14:10, the PC unlocked, the displays on, the right-most screen clear of everything but the desktop and no try window running; others on 11 to 54% of the job's CPUs, the PC 13 to 59%, the clock 2.4 to 3.4 GHz; from 12:57 to 13:56 Discord covered 98% of the screen and nothing was walked). The branch at 2b308abb7 with the probe ("A test window's motion", the probe), `budgets.ps1 -Scenario context-bar-widening -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace,lbterm::present::stats=trace,lbterm::compositor::lag=trace'` twelve times and `context-bar-pane-opening` three times, each a warden job with `--gpu-draw --lock lbterm-measure`, read with walk-read.ts. Every run was of the 4-miss kind: its widenings missed 4 to 6 at their start and its goings back 6 to 8, while `rail.row.grow` at the window's start missed 1 (2 in two runs, 0 in one); the two widening runs at 14:05 to 14:06 missed 6 to 16 a widening and the three pane-opening runs had the rail miss 3 to 7, so those five aren't counted below. No run of the other kind came, so the probe has no reading of it yet. What the ten counted runs (71 widenings, 71 goings back) say:

- **The widened bar's content is on screen with the first composed present, not after the gap.** The widening's frame presents three swapchains (`Over`, the layer over the panes, the bar's and the strip's; which of `Over`'s content changed on it wasn't read) and commits; DWM's first present to the screen goes up at -15.6 to +5.9 ms of the episode's opening. In the 20 widenings where the swapchains' statistics answered, all three of that frame's presents were on screen together at the refresh 11.3 to 20.1 ms after the opening by their own counts, read so by 21.9 to 33.0 ms; none later than that. In the other 51 none of the frame's swapchains answered at all (their statistics stayed at the present before through the episode, a frame at a time, never one swapchain alone: PITFALLS 338), so they say nothing either way. So DWM doesn't hold the composition for the bar's fresh content: drawing the widened bar ahead (What could go, below) has nothing to remove in this kind either.
- **Then DWM composes and doesn't present.** After that refresh come the 4 with no present to the screen, then two presents in one compositor frame (at about 71 ms in run b3's second widening) and every refresh after, as round 3 found. In the gap DWM's compositor frames go on a 160 Hz tick apart (the main screen's; 4 to 6 frame ids a refresh of DISPLAY1 in b3's), with nothing outstanding and the count still (round 3's raw frames of the kind, scratchpad r3s3's gw1, 6.25 ms apart): DWM has a frame of its tree each 6.25 ms and gives DISPLAY1 none of them, while the bar's slide, started in that commit, plays in its tree (a PrintWindow capture of 2026-11-09, a run of the same kind, had the bar's edge moving 5, 10, 11, 10 px about 20 ms apart from about 45 ms: a spring from rest, the motion `panel_open` gives 54 px). The commit went to DWM 1.1 to 3.2 ms after prepare (`lbterm::compositor::lag`; 38 ms on a window's first widening, its four visuals made), so nothing of LBTerm's is late.

Nothing of the bar's is presented in going back (row 9: its content is `CardDraw::Last`), so the probe has nothing of its own there.

DWM's overlay planes aren't turned off on this PC (no `OverlayTestMode` under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\Dwm`; an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4080, driver 32.0.16.2101), so the review's guess stands untested: that DWM puts the bar's swapchain on a hardware overlay plane and moves it there (no composed present for DISPLAY1, so the walk counts the refresh as missed, though the screen may show the bar moving), and composes it again when something else changes over or under it; the other kind would be runs where no plane is free for it. What could go, or is open (for the next stage and Lachlan):

- **The second slide: built in round 4's stage 6** (Lachlan's E9 of 2026-11-10; rows 5, 7 and 9). As it was proposed: the thin numbers' pane waited for the bar's slide and then slid out itself, two motions one after the other (about 840 ms to settle against the drawer's 503), and a clip whose right edge DWM animates with the bar's left edge, started in the same commit, would let it come out from under the bar's moving edge on the same slide: one motion, settled when the bar is. As built, the clip's edge isn't an animation of its own (`IDCompositionRectangleClip::SetRight` on a second curve): the thin pane's container, clip and all, sits in a visual (`Slid::follow`) whose offset plays the bar's own animation, handed to both in the bar's `drive`, and the thin pane slides its own width inside it. A second curve sampled from the same token for another distance ends apart from the bar's by the epsilon (`segments` ends a spring within `PIXEL_EPSILON` of its end: about 30 ms between 54 and 121 px on `panel_open`), so the clip would leave the bar's edge at the tail; the one animation can't (PITFALLS 353). At the perf script's size (a 68 px rail at 96 DPI) the thin pane's card then moves 133 px (the bar's 54, its own 68 and its shadow's 11 px reach, which its visual's bounds take in and its slide hides too) in the time the bar's edge moves 54, so its shadow comes out first and its card once the spring is 14% of its way, about 23 ms in; the strip, gliding its 121 px on the same spring (the bar's 54 and the card's 68 less the hairline), leads the card's left edge by 12 px of the remaining travel's share, about 10 px as the card comes out and none at rest, never over it (in round 3's order the strip, 121 px against the card's 79, ran under the widened bar and then over the thin card by up to 24 px). compositor/imp/tests/context_bar.rs holds every edge at every 5 ms. Going back stays as round 3 built it, from out. Walked and shown to `lbterm.ps1 look` in round 4's stage 6 (the readings above): one motion of 336 to 341 ms, missing main's one at its start.
