- **The bar's visual in the runs where it misses 4 (round 3's stage 2).** Trace before redesigning: in a run of each kind, the bar's swapchain's own statistics on the widening's frame (when its present went up, `IDXGISwapChain::GetFrameStatistics`, which present.rs's `watch` reads for an action's screen time), what else drew on the GPU then (the warden's jobs, LyreBird, other test windows) and which windows were on the screen. If DWM waits on the bar's fresh content before it moves the visual, the step is drawing the widened bar ahead (on the ring's frame, into its swapchain under an empty clip, so the widening's frame commits the clip and the slide with no present of the bar's): the one part of the probe's idea not tried, and stage 1's probe (the thin pane and the strip ahead) ran only in runs of the other kind, where there was nothing to gain. That the narrowing, which presents nothing of the bar's, misses more in the same runs says it may be DWM's work on the bar's visual itself rather than its content: then the step is what the bar's visual makes DWM do each refresh (its size and its clip over the window).
  What round 3's review and fix-up found of how to read it (2026-11-10, 04:00 to 04:30, the displays asleep, so nothing walked): (a) the review's guess, that DWM puts the bar's swapchain on a hardware overlay plane and moves it there with no composed present (which fits stage 2's raw frames: the vblank time moving, the count still, nothing outstanding, the bar alone), can't be read through `IDXGISwapChainMedia::GetFrameStatisticsMedia`'s `CompositionMode`: these composition swapchains answer E_NOINTERFACE for it (present.rs, `Watch`'s doc, 2026-10-30). (b) The bar's own swapchain's `GetFrameStatistics` can be read (the last present DWM put on screen: its count, its refresh and the sync refresh), and present.rs already polls a watched chain's and logs each poll at trace under `lbterm::present::stats`; but an action's watch is on one chain (`present::presented`: the chain of the action's pane, else `frame.chains.last()`), and on the widening's frame the last is the riding strip's (render/composited.rs gathers the frames in `acquire`, ..., `acquire_context_bar`, ... order, and `acquire_context_bar` in pane, bar, ride, thin order, nothing after it presenting then, by stage 1's lines), so the present watched for the widening is the strip's and the bar's is read nowhere today. A probe that also polls the bar's chain through the episode, logged beside the walk's frames, says whether the bar's widened content reached the screen with the composed present at about 1 ms or with the first present after the gap. (c) An overlay plane shows in the present mode ETW gives each swapchain (PresentMon's `Hardware Composed: Independent Flip` against `Composed: Flip`); PresentMon isn't on this PC (2026-11-10), and the Windows Performance Toolkit's `wpr.exe` is (on PATH), whose GPU profile records the same DxgKrnl and DWM events but runs elevated, so a trace by it is Lachlan's to run. (d) The kind isn't Lachlan's input: his last input was about 02:18 (the displays went to sleep at about 03:19 on a 3600 s timeout), and stage 3's runs at 02:09, 02:13 and 02:24 were of the 4-miss kind and 02:20 and 02:26 of the other. Each walk of it compares with main's `rail.row.grow` at the window's start and labels its run's kind (PITFALLS 306).
  What round 4's stage 4 found with (b)'s probe (above; runs of the 4-miss kind only, none of the other came): the bar's widened content is on screen with the first composed present, 11 to 20 ms after the opening, so DWM doesn't wait on it, and drawing the widened bar ahead is out for this kind too. The gap is DWM giving DISPLAY1 no present for 4 refreshes while its frames go on at 160 Hz and the bar's slide plays in its tree; going back, the same from 23 to 55 ms into the bar's slide to its end. What that points to, in order, none of it built:
  1. **Read what the screen showed in the gap, before changing the flow.** If DWM moves the bar on an overlay plane, the screen may show every refresh of the slide and the misses are the walk's, which counts DWM's composed presents only. Lachlan's elevated trace settles it (the stage's report has the command for him: `wpr -start GPU -start DesktopComposition -filemode`, one walk of `context-bar-widening`, `wpr -stop <file>.etl`), read with no elevation by the Windows Performance Toolkit installed here (`xperf -i <file>.etl -o <file>.txt -a dumper`, or WPA): Microsoft-Windows-DxgKrnl's `MMIOFlipMultiPlaneOverlay` events on DISPLAY1's VidPn source (0) at each vblank of the gap, with a plane other than DWM's whose destination's left edge steps as the spring does, would be the overlay; no flip for that source in the gap would be a screen left as it was. Without the trace, his call too (system-wide, elevated): overlay planes off (`OverlayTestMode` 5 under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\Dwm`, DWM restarted) and the walk again; the 4-miss kind going with them would say it's the plane.
  2. **If it's a plane: the measure, not the motion.** Nothing in DComp's statistics has a plane's flips, so the walk can't count them; the budget's `missed` would be judged only on what DWM composes, and a motion DWM puts on a plane said apart (a cue for it would have to come from outside DComp: the trace above, or DXGI's `IDXGIOutput3::CheckOverlaySupport` for the format, which says only whether one could be). No change to the bar.
  What round 4's stage 5 found, which answers all three (below): DxgKrnl's flips put no overlay on the screen and nothing on it in the gap, and their dirty rectangles put the bar's moving edge past the screen's right edge until then. The test window hung 70 px past the screen, so it was 1, not 2 or 3: nothing of the bar's visual, its make or its place in the four is DWM's to treat apart, and nothing in the flow needs restructuring for it. Kept as it was written, for the record:
  3. **If the screen showed nothing new: what DWM does with the bar's visual that it doesn't with the thin pane's**, which has the same make (a `Slid`) and presents through. What LBTerm decides of it: its place on top of the four, over the ride and the pane; a swapchain drawn once and then only slid (`OffsetX` on its slider); its size, the widened bar's whole height. The restructuring to try first is the one Lachlan's E9 asks for anyway, the thin numbers' pane coming out from under the bar's moving edge in one slide, if it puts the bar's card and the thin pane in one visual and one slide: the bar would then move as the thin pane moves now. Changing the bar's slide to a clip's edge over a still card is the other, but its words would be uncovered in place rather than slide in flat, a look Lachlan judges.
- **The widening's frame presents three swapchains: out.** The idea was to draw and present the thin numbers' pane and the riding strip on the ring's frame, so the widening's frame presented only the bar. Round 3's probe built it and walked it: it presents two now, not three (row 6), and the probe's one went up a refresh later and missed as many (above). Its start missed the one refresh main's motions do in stage 1's runs, so it needed no redesign there; that holds only for runs of the other kind: stage 2 and stage 5 found the start missing 4 or 5 in runs of the 4-miss kind, on every build (stage 5: every window of 03:01 to 03:28, the CPU quiet), and stage 1's probe was walked only in runs of the other kind, so drawing ahead is out only for them ("The bar's visual in the runs where it misses 4", above, has what's left of it).
- **The narrowing's tail: end a timed slide at its last pixel: out.** Stage 1 proposed ending a timed slide in pixels where its curve comes within half a pixel of `to`, taking the tail's misses for refreshes with nothing new to show. Round 3's stage 3 found they aren't (above): the redesigned going back (row 9) presents through the same eased tail in most runs of the other kind, and a linear slide back stopped presenting with 19 px to go in one of the 4-miss kind. What's left is the 4-miss kind's, with the widening's start: "The bar's visual in the runs where it misses 4", above.
- **The walk's base (PITFALLS 301): built in round 3's stage 2** (`Walk::painted`, `Sample::presented_at`; "A test window's motion", steps 4 and 7, and PITFALLS 301 and 302 have it as it runs). As it was proposed: until the walk stops counting from a present that came before the episode opened, any scenario whose action comes after a rest (this one, the pane's opening, the cost pane's) has a `missed` median that's 1 or 6 by chance, so no before-and-after of these motions can be judged on it. The walk should take a frame as the motion's going up only if its present came after the paint that opened the episode began, and re-base on any earlier one instead of starting on it (`Walk::step`, lbterm-extras/src/present.rs). Not the opening itself: the episode opens at the paint's end (`frame_end`), and a right present's vblank time comes up to 15 ms before that line, so the walk needs the paint's start, which `Present::motion` wasn't given then. That's a change to how the walk reads, not to any number.
- **The first frame's jump.** The sections' heights change at the widening's first frame (a 3 px floor at rest, a line widened), drawn at once rather than moved: a change of layout, not motion, and the one thing in the widening that isn't flat.

## The context pill's press, to the cost pane's count and its fade

What runs when Lachlan presses the context pill (lbterm/CONTEXT.md, "The cost pane"; his 7c of 2026-11-09), from the code of feat/context-bar's second round, stage 5, and round 5's stage 2 (2026-11-14), which made an opening where nothing moved ask nothing and paint only the press's frame. Nothing of it runs while the pane is shut: no read, no layout, no frame (the thread, once a press has started it, waits on its channel); the one thing that does is the summaries thread counting each write of a session's `<sid>.cost.json` it hears, which it reads none of (below the second table). Steps 3's ask and 5 to 9 are LBTerm's own count, which runs only with `lbterm_context.count_cost` on (the pill's, since round 4's stage 1, as the pane hangs from the pill; the bar's from round 3's stage 4, off unless asked for since then, Lachlan's 2b of 2026-11-10); off, the pane shows the Claude project's split (`@Claude:cost`), and the rows below the table say what runs instead.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The press: `lbterm_cost_mouse` takes it (before the projects pad's and the strip's items), `Hover::open_now`, invalidates; the release is taken too (`pressed`) | render/cost.rs, from mouseevent.rs, GUI thread | a press | a compare and a flag | yes |
| 2 | The paint: `lbterm_context_refresh` sees `Shown::open` change and the strip is built again with the pill lit (`lbterm_prompt_row`, the pill's kept room laid out with `$00.00`, the pill, the pad) | render/context.rs, render/strip.rs, GUI thread | a frame as it opens and as it shuts | the strip's build, as for any change on it (a few elements laid out) | yes: the pill shows it's open |
| 3 | `paint_lbterm_cost`: the pill's rect from `ui_items`, the session from the pill; the pill's dollars to the cent from `summaries::get`; with the count on (`config::lbterm::counts_cost`) the session and the dollars compared with what the pane last asked for while open (`CostState::counting`, forgotten as it shuts, with the count off and at a reload: nothing hears the transcripts while it's shut, so an opening asks); they differ, so `cost::want` sends the session down the channel (`Counter::ask`), starting `lbterm-cost` the first time | render/cost.rs, wezterm-gui/src/cost.rs, GUI thread | each paint while it's open; the ask as it opens and when the dollars moved | a map lookup and a compare; a channel send | yes, with the count on: only the thread can see whether a transcript moved, and it answers nothing when none did (8) |
| 4 | Nothing counted yet (the first opening on a conversation): `cost::pane(None)` lays out `Counting…` (one word); after it the count kept shows, laid out once (`LaidFrom` holds its `Arc`), until a new one comes. The card drawn, the visual (`Target::Objectives(4)`) faded in by the compositor on `pane_enter` | render/cost.rs, compositor.rs | once | a word | yes |
| 5 | On `lbterm-cost`: every ask waiting taken at once, one count a session. `read_on`: the transcript found (`find_transcript`: a `read_dir` of the transcripts folder and a stat a project folder, the first time only; after it the kept path is stat'd), then `Fold::read` on from its offset: open, its size, and only when it grew, seek, read to the end, parse only the lines holding `"assistant"` or `compact_boundary` (`memmem`), each a `serde_json` parse. It says whether the fold moved: a line taken, or a file written again (shorter, even empty), since round 5's stage 2 | wezterm-gui/src/cost.rs, lbterm-extras/src/cost.rs, its own thread | an ask | the first time the whole transcript (his biggest, 13.7 MB); after it what was appended since, and an open and a size when nothing was | yes, the first; after it only what's new |
| 6 | The subagents: `agent_transcripts` walks `<sid>/` five folders deep (a `read_dir` a folder: about 15 folders and 70 files for a conversation that ran 13 workflows), and each `.jsonl` is read on from its own offset as in 5; a subagent not read before is a move | the same | an ask | a walk of the folder each ask, an open and a size a file; the reads only what each file gained | the walk yes (a new subagent can start any time, and nothing watches the folder while the pane is shut); not traced whether a watch would be cheaper |
| 7 | `count` (`cost::cost`): the parts summed from every fold's requests, every request put in time order (a sort of all of them, again each count), the running total, the marks; only when 5 or 6 moved something, or no count is kept: an opening where no transcript moved makes none | lbterm-extras/src/cost.rs, the same thread | an ask that moved | the sort is n log n over every request the conversation made (thousands for a long one) | the sums yes; the sort could keep each fold's order and merge only what's new (What could go) |
| 8 | Compared with the answer kept for the session (`Counted::same`: the same `Arc`, else the parts, steps and marks by value, or the same words why): the same (nothing moved, or lines that spent nothing), nothing is stored and nothing repainted (round 5's stage 2); else stored by session as an `Arc` behind a lock, and `spawn_into_main_thread` repaints the windows that asked and those with a pane open on the session with the count off (`SHOWING`) | the same, then the GUI thread | an ask | a compare (thousands of steps for a long conversation, microseconds); a lock and a notify when it differs | yes |
| 9 | The repaint, only for an answer that differs (8): `LaidFrom` differs (another `Arc`), so `cost::pane` lays the pane out: the table's words (about fifty), the bar's parts, the graph's grid, ticks and line (a pass over the plot's pixel columns, each a binary search of the steps, merged into runs: up to two fills a column) and the marks, the words placed by their widths (the total, the graph's dollars, its times, its marks' names: about a dozen, more with more ticks) measured as they're laid out to be drawn (`lbterm_context_width`, one `compute_element` each, since stage 6 of the second round), the rest counted in columns | render/cost.rs, lbterm-extras/src/cost.rs, GUI thread | a count | proportional to the graph's width, not the requests | yes, once a count |
| 10 | Each paint while it shows: the card (`lbterm_objectives_card`), a quad a fill (`lbterm_menu_rounded`), the words through render/words.rs (their quads kept while the same) into z-layers 5 to 7, and the compositor presents its visual only when its content changed | render/cost.rs, compositor.rs, GUI thread | each frame the window paints while it's open or fading | up to two quads a graph column; nothing laid out | yes, while it shows |
| 11 | A new request while it's open: Terminal's statusline writes `usage.json` once Claude Code has recorded it, `summaries` repaints the window showing that session (the pill's), the pill's dollars move, and step 3 asks again: steps 5 to 9 for what's new | summaries thread, then as above | a request (his or a subagent's) | as steps 5 to 9, the reads only what's new | yes |
| 12 | Shutting: a press on the pill (at once) or the pointer leaving the pill and the pane (`COST_CLOSE_MS`, its timer); the strip built again with the pill unlit; the visual fades out on `pane_exit`, the last pane drawn meanwhile (`CardDraw::Last`), and a wake at the fade's end puts it away (`lbterm_cost_wake`, below) | render/cost.rs, GUI thread, the compositor | a shutting | a frame or two | yes |

**With the count off** (the default; the Claude project's split since round 4's stage 3, 2026-11-10, Lachlan's 2b; round 3's stage 4 showed only the pill's dollars and window). Steps 1, 2, 8, 10 and 12 run as above; in place of 3 to 7, 9 and 11:

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3' | `paint_lbterm_cost`: the pill's rect and session as in 3, the session's `usage.json` from `summaries::get` (the pill's dollars and the window, already in memory for the pill), and `counts_cost` false. Open, the pane follows the session's split (`follow_split`, telling `cost::showing` the window and the session when they change: in `SHOWING` while open, out as it shuts, when the pill goes, and when the window drops, `CostState`'s `Drop`). Then `cost::want`, each paint while open: `summaries::split_written(sid)`, how many times the summaries thread has heard `<sid>.cost.json` written (it reads none of it), compared with the count the split was last asked for at (`ASKED`, by session, shared by every window); the same, nothing is sent, which is every opening on a split not written since and every paint after its ask; else the count is noted and a `Source::Published(lbterm_summaries)` job sent (`Counter::ask`), starting `lbterm-cost` at the first. Then `cost::get(sid, false)`, which answers only the Claude project's way. Until round 5's stage 2 a shut pane forgot what it asked and asked at every opening, keyed on the pill's cents | render/cost.rs, wezterm-gui/src/cost.rs, GUI thread | each paint while it's open; the ask at a conversation's first opening and when its split was written since | four locks and a compare; a channel send when written | yes |
| 4' | Nothing read yet (the first opening on a conversation): `cost::note(READING)`, one word; after that the last answer kept for the session shows until the new one comes | render/cost.rs, GUI thread | once a conversation | a word | yes |
| 5' | On `lbterm-cost`, only when asked (3', 11'): every ask waiting taken at once, one read a session: `cost::read_published` checks the session's id (`restore::is_session_id`, never a path from anything else), reads `<lbterm_summaries>/<sid>.cost.json` whole (one open and read; 1 KB for a short conversation, 78 KB for his longest of 2026-11-10, 93 spenders and 1996 steps) and `published` parses it with `serde_json` (`v` first: another version is the words saying which, and no split), then sums the spenders under each row, groups the workflows by `run`, takes a mark at each subagent's and each run's first `start`, and sorts the marks and the steps; no transcript is found, opened or read, and no `Fold` is kept (`answer`, `a_published_answer_reads_no_transcript`) | wezterm-gui/src/cost.rs, lbterm-extras/src/cost.rs, its own thread | an ask | a file read whole and parsed each ask, not what it gained: the file is rewritten whole each time (a rename) | yes: it's the split; reading on from an offset isn't possible in a file written whole |
| 6' | Compared with the answer kept, as 8 (`Counted::same`: the split's parts, steps and marks by value, or the same words why); the same (a file written again with the same split), nothing stored or repainted; else stored by session as `Counted::Split` (or `Unpublished(why)`: no file, which is not yet published, never $0; a version it doesn't read; what went wrong) and the windows that asked and those with a pane open on the session repainted | the same, then the GUI thread | an ask | a compare; a lock and a notify when it differs | yes |
| 7' | The repaint: `LaidFrom` differs, so the split is laid out by `cost::pane`, as 9 lays out the count (its total the file's `usd`, to the cent as the pill has it, not its parts' sum), or without one by `cost::usage_pane`: the pill's dollars, `this conversation, at list price`, `200k context window` and why there's no split (`NOT_PUBLISHED`, `NO_SUMMARIES`, the version's words) | render/cost.rs, lbterm-extras/src/cost.rs, GUI thread | an answer | as 9, or four words | yes |
| 11' | The split moves while it's open: Terminal's statusline writes `usage.json` and then, 15 to 33 ms later in the same run (mtimes of 14 conversations' latest writes, 2026-11-10), `cost.json` (`rememberUsage`, then `rememberCost`). The summaries thread hears both in one batch (its 60 ms `SETTLE` from the first) or the second in the next; it counts the split's write (`split_written`, before anything wakes) and tells `Changed::Split` first, on its own thread: `cost::split_written` finds the panes open on the session in `SHOWING` and calls `want` for each, which sees the count moved and sends the job, with no frame of its own (the first window's ask covers the rest, `ASKED` being by session). Then the cents' `Changed::Usage` repaints the pill's windows as before, and that paint's `want` finds the split asked for at its count already. 5' and 6' follow, and only a split that differs repaints | summaries thread, then lbterm-cost, then the GUI thread | a request | a lock and a map's add; as 5' to 7' | yes: the pane reads the split on its own write, so one written past `SETTLE` shows when it's written (PITFALLS 323), not at the next cent |

The summaries thread hears every `cost.json` write in the folder too (a folder watch; the statusline writes one each time a conversation's split changes, every request of every conversation on the PC since Terminal's 073e165) and reads none of it. Before round 5's stage 2 it dropped them (`parse_name` knows no `.cost.json`); since, for a session asked for (a tab's), it counts the write (`split_of`, a lock and a map's add, `Summaries::split_written`, one more for every session asked for when the folder came, went or its watch failed, since a write may have gone unheard) and tells `Changed::Split`, which wezterm-gui's summaries.rs gives to `cost::split_written` on that thread: with no pane open on the session it returns there, so no window wakes and nothing is read. The thread already woke and slept its `SETTLE` for each write. The reload: `cost::configured` forgets every split's count (`ASKED`), since another summaries folder's writes count from none, so an open pane or the next opening reads it once more, and an answer the same as the one kept repaints nothing.

The thread is one way's for its life (`Counter::switch`): an ask the other way, or a reload that turns the count on or off (`config_was_reloaded` → `lbterm_cost_configure` → `cost::configured`), drops its channel, so it ends at its next wait with the folds it kept, and empties `COUNTS`; an answer a thread made the other way after that is never shown (`get` takes only its own way's), and the open pane's `asked` holds the way it asked, so it asks the new way at its next paint. Without `lbterm_summaries` nothing is sent: the answer is `NO_SUMMARIES`, at once.

**The wake** (`lbterm_cost_wake`, render/cost.rs; traced in round 5's stage 2). Armed at the end of every `paint_lbterm_cost` twice, with the fades' settle (`objectives_showing`: the latest end of any of the objectives' five cards' fades in flight, this pane's or another's) and with the hover's deadline (`Hover::deadline`: a closing grace's end; `Open` has none, and the pane is only opened by `open_now`, so never `Opening`'s), and from `lbterm_cost_mouse` and `lbterm_cost_mouse_leave` with the deadline. One wake is kept: a call is dropped while one due no later is still ahead. Its timer (`smol::Timer` on the GUI's executor through `promise::spawn`) notifies the window at `at`, which ticks the hover (shutting the pane when its grace is up) and invalidates, whatever changed: a frame. At the press's frame no fade is in flight yet (the compositor starts `pane_enter` in that frame's `prepare_objectives`, after the paint), so nothing is armed; a paint during the fade-in saw its end and armed a wake there, whose frame drew the same pane again. Until round 5's stage 2 every opening had such a paint, the count's answer (`wezterm-gui/src/cost.rs`, `repaint`), so the wake was the opening's fourth frame (PERF.md, "The context bar's round 4": 75 of each, none on 0ffd7619b). Since, the settle arms it only while the pane is shut (`!open`): at a fade-out's end the last pane drawn goes (`CardDraw::None`), and open, nothing drawn changes at a fade's end. The paint that follows a shutting press comes before its fade-out starts too, so it arms nothing either; a paint during the fade-out does, and without one nothing in render/cost.rs wakes at its end, the visual left at no alpha until the window next paints (whether the compositor's `prepare_objectives` asks for a frame then isn't traced; in a test window the state file's own wake, `lbterm_state_wake`, paints there). Unchanged by round 5's stage 2. How each close leaves the hover (the review of 2026-11-14, from lbterm-panel's `Hover` and render/cost.rs's press): a press on the pill while the pane is open calls `Hover::close`, which goes straight to `Closed` with no deadline, so a press-shut arms no hover wake at all, only the press's own `lbterm_invalidate`; a leave goes through `Closing`, whose deadline (the grace, `Hover::new(grace, grace)`) wakes one paint, the one that turns it `Closed` and starts the fade-out, so it comes before the fade-out as the press's paint does. Neither gives a paint at the fade-out's end. Nothing else puts the faded card away: the compositor's `put_away_faded` (compositor.rs) puts away only panes and their outlines once their fade-out has ended, never an objectives card, so a cost pane that faded out with no paint during its fade keeps its visual and its last quads, at opacity 0, until the window next paints for anything (whether DWM skips composing a visual at opacity 0 isn't traced).

**What an opening runs now** (round 5's stage 2, from the code; measured by round 5's stage 3, below). With the count off, where nothing was written since its last ask: 1, 2, 3' (the paint's `want` sends nothing), the pane laid out again only if its place changed, else drawn from what was laid out: the press's frame alone, and nothing read. With the count on: 1, 2, 3 (an ask), and on `lbterm-cost` 5 and 6 with nothing taken (an open and a size a file, the subagents' walk), no count made, nothing stored or repainted: the press's frame alone. Round 4 measured 4 frames and 10 messages against 0ffd7619b's 2 and 6, and the GUI thread 5.19 ms against 2.89, with the count on. Round 5's stage 3 measured this against the merge's build, which still asked again (PERF.md, "The context bar's round 5"): 2 frames and 6 messages against 4 and 10 with the count on and with it off, the GUI thread 3.22 ms against 5.63 and 3.24 against 6.48, the time to the press's frame the same (about 2.1 ms). The two frames are the press's and the test window's state file's; the latter is its own wake (`lbterm_state_wake`, `PAST_THE_END`, 20 ms) after the fade, the first frame with nothing animating now that no wake of the pane's paints at the fade's end, so `settled_ms` reads 20 ms later than the merge's build (PITFALLS 370). Lachlan's window paints the press's frame alone.

**What could go.** Step 7 sorts every request of the conversation each count; each `Fold`'s requests are already in time order within their file, so a count could keep the merged steps and merge in only the requests the reads just took. Step 6 walks the subagents' folders each ask, an opening with nothing new included: a few dozen `read_dir`s and an open a file, cheap beside the reads, but a watch on `<sid>/` (lbterm_extras::watch) would say when a file came; it would also wake for every record Claude Code writes while the pane is shut, which no part of the pane does now. Neither is measured, and neither runs unless the pane is open. With the count on an opening still asks (3), since nothing hears the transcripts while it's shut: what it costs is 5 and 6 finding nothing, off the GUI thread. The count's own reading is the Claude Code producer of the pane's split and goes when Claude Code tabs retire (lbterm/CONTEXT.md, "What stays LBTerm's when a harness takes Claude Code's place"), so it isn't given a watch of its own here.

## A test window's motion, from an episode's opening to the budget's `missed`

How a test window says whether every refresh of its screen got a new frame while its motion played, from the frame that starts the motion to the budget runner's verdict on `missed` (PERF.md, "Frames missed during motion"; the budget of 0 on every action). Off in Lachlan's window: all of it runs only while `lbterm_extras::present` is on (`LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING` in a test window's environment, which the budget runner gives every action, or a `metrics --present on` request). Traced on 2026-11-08 from the code, a trace of the walk (`budgets.ps1 -Scenario tab-menu-opening -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` and `rail-opening` the same, on the branch's build with the trace and on the installed main's; the right-most screen was covered every time, so no episode was walked), and every screen's counts read frame by frame (perf/screen-counts.ps1, 20 s, no test window open). All on the GUI thread but step 9, which is the runner's.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A frame's end hands over what the compositor has in flight (each animation's name and end), and the first frame with anything still to end opens an episode; later frames extend its names and its settle (`Episode::extend`) and count its paints and those over a refresh (`Episode::paint`) | wezterm-gui/src/present.rs `frame_end`, `Present::motion` | every frame while present timing is on; an opening an episode | a map lookup and a loop over what's in flight, a frame | yes, for the budget's motion figures |
| 2 | The screen's compositor target, once a monitor: the window's monitor (`MonitorFromWindow`, nearest), its adapter and VidPn source (`source_of`: `GetMonitorInfoW`, `D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromGdiDisplayName`), and the target among the newest completed frame's (`DCompositionGetStatistics`) with that display adapter and source, with one refresh of it (`vblankDuration`); kept by monitor handle for the process's life. This PC's four screens are sources 0 to 3 of one adapter, a target each; DISPLAY1, the right-most portrait screen where the test windows open, is source 0 | `Present::screen` | once a monitor a process | a few system calls, once | yes |
| 3 | Whether others cover the window: the windows above it in the z-order, sampled at 16 by 16 points of its part on its screen, at most every 250 ms; 95% covered is hidden, and a covered episode is never walked (`covered`), so it measures no `missed` (since round 4's stage 5 of feat/context-bar, budgets.ps1 first puts the window all on its screen, `Move-TestWindowOnScreen`, once it has its pane and its rail: the cover check samples only the part on its screen, so a window past the screen's edge read 0% covered while its right 70 px, the context bar's, showed on no screen: PITFALLS 351) | `Present::hidden`, `covered` | once an episode opening | a z-order walk | yes: a covered window's screen gets no frame of its motion, so every refresh would read as missed (PERF.md, "Hidden windows") |
| 4 | Its base: the newest completed compositor frame (`DCompositionGetFrameId`, `COMPOSITION_FRAME_ID_COMPLETED`, which names a frame only once a later one has begun, so on a quiet PC it's the frame before the newest: PITFALLS 301) and the screen's counts as of it (`Clock::sample`: `DCompositionGetStatistics` and `DCompositionGetTargetStatistics`), and the walk made with the vblank as its refresh (`Walk::new`, none without one) and the paint's start (`Walk::painted`: the QPC of the `Instant` `frame_start` noted for the frame that opened it, so no walk for a frame whose start wasn't seen) | `Present::motion`, `Clock::sample` | once an episode | three calls into dcomp.dll | yes |
| 5 | Its looks: one timer for the soonest of the settle + 30 ms, a refresh apart after that until the walk's done or 200 ms have gone, and every 800 ms while it plays (the menu's "Opening, measured whole", step 6) | `Present::arm`, `wake`, `walk_all`; lbterm-extras `Episode::next_look`, `finished` | one look an episode while the PC composes, up to a dozen when it doesn't | a poll and a walk each | yes |
| 6 | A walk: every compositor frame since the last walked, to the newest completed (at most 240 back; further, it carries on from there, `skip_to`, and says `partial`), each read with `Clock::sample` and stepped (`Walk::step`). The compositor makes a frame a tick of the fastest screen's refresh only while something on some screen changes: 2,421 frames in 20 s with Lachlan at the PC (11:45), 845 in 20 s and 14 in 2.4 s when the PC was quieter (11:28) | `walk`, `Clock::sample` | per look, over the frames since the last | two calls into dcomp.dll a frame; metrics' `present.cost` (`walks`, `walk_ms`) | yes |
| 7 | Each frame stepped: before the first whose present count (`presentedStats.presentCount`: every present DWM made to that screen, whoever's) has moved from the base's, nothing is counted; a frame whose count moved but whose own present to the screen (the target's `presentTime`, `Sample::presented_at`, the QPC of DWM's present call, 0 in a frame with none) came before the paint's start (step 4) becomes the base instead, since nothing that paint made can be up before it began (the rest's present on a quiet PC, PITFALLS 301; until round 3's stage 2 of feat/context-bar such a frame was taken as the motion's start). Not `presentedStats.time`: at a motion's first frame the count moves while that time stays at the refresh before, for up to three compositor frames (PITFALLS 302), so a cut by it takes the motion's own first frame for the rest's, as the cut's first build did; the first after that whose count moved is where the motion went up, its `first_ms` that frame's `presentedStats.time` (`first_ms` from its vblank time, `lead` in refreshes since the base). From it each frame adds the refreshes since the frame before by the screen's vblank times (`refreshes_between`: the `presentedStats.time` difference over the vblank, rounded) and the presents by the count; `missed` is the whole span's refreshes less its presents (a frame's counts can come a frame late), and a frame begun after the settle ends the walk. The screen's own refresh counter (`presentedStats.refreshCount`) is added up beside them as `counted`, judged by nothing. **Until 2026-11-08 the refreshes were the counter's**, which add up to the counter at the last frame less the counter at the first present, and the counter jumps at a present on this PC's screens (forward by thousands in every unlocked run of 2026-11-07, back by 4 to 61 on 2026-11-08) while the times stay a refresh apart: every `missed` judged on 2026-11-07 was the counter's jump, not frames DWM dropped (PITFALLS 166) | lbterm-extras/src/present.rs `Walk::step`, `refreshes_between` | per frame of the episode | arithmetic | yes |
| 8 | Finished (`Episode::finished`): what it came to (`Episode::done`, `MotionDone`), its line logged under `lbterm::present` at debug (in the form "motion menu.tab, 222 ms: 15 refreshes, 2 missed, first frame up at 4.3 ms; ...", "; the screen's refresh counter said N" when the counter disagrees, or "not measured, another window was over it"), added to the totals (`Totals::motion`: the measured episodes' refreshes, `counted`, presents and missed, and by name) | `Present::finish`; lbterm-extras `Totals::motion` | once an episode | a line when logged | yes |
| 9 | The runner: each run's `metrics` reply (reset before it) under `present.motion`; `ConvertTo-ActionSample` takes `missed`, `refreshes`, `refreshes_counted` and `motion_first_ms` only when an episode was measured (`measured` 1 or more); `Test-BudgetScenario` holds the runs' median `missed` to its budget, 0, and a metric no run measured is "nothing measured (not judged)" (PITFALLS 182) | lbterm/budgets.psm1 | per run, per scenario | none in the window | yes |

The trace, with `lbterm::present::walk` at trace in the window's log (wezterm-gui/src/present.rs): each target step 2 finds with the monitor's adapter and source, each episode's opening with its base frame, whether it was covered and when its paint began (`painted at Q`, or `unseen`), every frame step 6 reads with its raw counts (the frame's start, target time and period; the screen's outstanding presents, present time and vblank; `presentedStats`' and `completedStats`' present count, refresh counter, virtual refresh counter and time), and each walk's span and running totals. With `lbterm::present` at debug, step 8's line.

The probe (round 4's stage 4 of feat/context-bar, 2026-11-10), with `lbterm::present::stats` at trace as well: what each visual's swapchain says DWM put on screen, beside the walk's frames. An action's watch reads one swapchain (its pane's, else the frame's last: the riding strip's on the context bar's widening), so no other visual's was read. On the GUI thread, all of it in wezterm-gui/src/present.rs:

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | A frame's presents and commit done: each swapchain it presented kept by its visual's `Target` (`Probed`; a visual whose swapchain was made again read from its new one), with a line `probe chain [<target>] presented <id> at <qpc>` (`GetLastPresentCount`, the QPC after the commit) | `presented`, `Present::probe_presented` | each frame | a cast and a call a swapchain, and a line | only to probe |
| P2 | Each kept swapchain of a window an episode plays in read (`IDXGISwapChain::GetFrameStatistics`), a line for each answer that differs from the last: `probe chain [<target>] at <qpc> (looked <qpc>): present <n> at refresh <r>, sync refresh <s> at <qpc>`, or the HRESULT | `Present::probe`, from `frame_start`, `wake` and a timer `arm` sets `PROBE_EVERY` (4 ms) on while an episode plays where it has any | a read a swapchain at each frame's start, each wake and each tick of the timer | a call a swapchain; not timed | only to probe |
| P3 | walk-read.ts puts each line among the episode's refreshes by its QPC time, a change at the refresh its own counts give (the sync refresh's time less `s - r` refreshes), with when it was read and read before | .claude/tools/walk-read.ts, `probes` | per log | none in the window | only to read it |

Off (no `lbterm::present::stats` at trace) nothing of it runs but `probing()`'s level check at each frame's presents and each `arm`. The runs of 13:56 to 14:10 (the context bar's section has them): a frame's swapchains answer together or not at all, 20 of 71 widenings' frames answering, so a present whose statistics never answer says nothing (PITFALLS 338). What the answers are on this PC (2026-11-10, DISPLAY1, a covered run, 12:57): a composition swapchain says DXGI_ERROR_FRAME_STATISTICS_DISJOINT (`0x887a000b`) at its first read and all zeros at the next, and from then on, for the last present DWM put on screen, its count with `SyncRefreshCount` equal to `PresentRefreshCount` and `SyncQPCTime` that refresh's QPC time: the sync pair is the present's own refresh, so the answer changes only when another present of that swapchain goes up, and says when, to the refresh, whenever it's read. The timer's ticks come about 15.6 ms apart in a test window (async-io's timer ends on Windows' tick), so the reads bound a change to a refresh or so, and the counts give the refresh.

What every screen's counts do, frame by frame (perf/screen-counts.ps1, 2026-11-08): `presentCount` moves by one a present on every screen; `time` moves on at every refresh DWM sees, a present or not, a refresh apart (within a fraction of a millisecond); `refreshCount` agrees with `time` on the main screen (DISPLAY2, 160 Hz, all 2,421 frames), lags it by one at each present on DISPLAY3, goes back 4 to 61 at 18 frames in 20 s on DISPLAY4 (393 refreshes by it against 1,200 by its times), and went back 39 at a present after a quiet spell on DISPLAY1, where the test windows open. A frame with a present outstanding (`outstandingPresents` 1) can carry a `refreshCount` ahead of the screen's, which the next frame takes back. `completedStats` is filled only at a frame where a present completed.

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- **The walk's start: the first frame's count is paired with the refresh before.** At a motion's first compositor frame the count moves while `presentedStats.time` stays at the refresh before (PITFALLS 302), and step 7 takes that frame as the motion's going up, counts refreshes from its time and presents only from the frame after it. The two raw traces of 2026-11-10 (round 3's stage 2): the context bar's widening, the count up 2 at a frame begun 0.1 ms in (DWM's presents at 2.6 ms, two outstanding) with the time -9.1, then 24.2 with the count the same, then one present at each refresh (40.9 to 90.9), which the walk counts as 2 refreshes with no present and makes 1 of back where the last frame has one outstanding; the rail's `rail.row.grow`, main's code, the count up at 4.1 and 6.0 ms with the time 2.1 (a refresh before either present), then 35.4 with the count the same. Read as DWM's presents (each outstanding one shown at a refresh after it), every refresh from the first present's has a new image, and the one refresh main's motions are counted to miss, the second after their first frame (PERF.md, "Measured on a window that could be seen"), is the walk's. The redesign: count from the refresh the first present goes up at (the first vblank after its `presentTime`) and the presents from the count before it, and say the ones outstanding at the last frame apart. Not built: it changes what `missed` says for every motion, main's included, and the bar it's held to; for Lachlan and the next stage.
- The walk by times on a test window that can be seen, before and after: not measured. On 2026-11-08 the right-most screen was covered (DashboardsBox and Discord, 100% of it), so every episode was `covered` and `missed` unjudged, as in 48 of the 50 rounds of 2026-11-07 23:05 (PITFALLS 182). Uncovered, `budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening,inbox-opening -Against <main's build>` says whether the menus' fades miss refreshes, with `refreshes_counted` beside `refreshes` to show the counter's jumps; main's build, which still counts by the counter, can't be judged on `missed` against it.
- What made the right-most screen's counter jump forward from 2026-11-07 (it hadn't in any run kept from 2026-10-30 to 11-04) isn't known: not LBTerm's code (main's installed build jumped too); a display mode, driver or screen change that week would. Nothing here turns on it now.
- Steps 2 to 7 run only in test windows, a few system calls a frame of motion; none of them does work it doesn't need.
- An action's time to the screen (`screen_ms`) is read from another counter, DXGI's frame statistics for the swapchain that shows it (`lbterm_extras::present::seen`: the sync refresh's time, less the refreshes from the present's refresh to the sync's, a refresh each). If those refresh counts jump at a present as `presentedStats.refreshCount` does, a screen time would be off by the jump; not looked at frame by frame, but not by thousands: the 38 screen times of perf/mux-client's `budgets -Against` of 2026-11-07 20:13, on the screen and the day the counter jumped by thousands, came 12.8 to 26.3 ms after their presents, one or two refreshes.
